# wardHub Training Quiz - Question Bank (for proofreading)

> **Purpose:** a just-for-fun revision quiz for inpatient mental health staff. **Nothing is tracked or scored on any record** - it is a private learning aid, not an assessment.
> **Status:** DRAFT for Mike to fact-check and sign off.
> **This file is auto-generated** from the question batches in `src/lib/data/quiz/research-*.json`. To grow the bank, add another batch file and regenerate. Edit questions in the JSON (or tell Claude) so this stays the single source of truth.

Every question has a **difficulty**, a plain-English **rationale**, and a **source**.

**Difficulty rubric** (kept consistent so it does not drift):
- **Easy** - a single recall fact any ward staff should know.
- **Medium** - applies a fact, or recognises a scenario.
- **Hard** - multi-step reasoning, a specific threshold/timing, or less commonly known detail.

Correct answer marked with **[correct]**. Acronyms are expanded in full on first use.

---

## About the trust-policy questions

These were mined directly from the trust's policy library (the local document dump, not the public
web). The `Source` line on each question names the actual policy, with its issue/review date
where the document stated one, so anyone can go and check the original on FOCUS.

**Two things to watch when signing these off:**
1. **Review dates.** Policies are reissued. Any question tagged with a review-date warning should
   be re-checked against the current version before it goes live.
2. **No internal contact details.** By project rule, no internal phone numbers, extensions, bleeps
   or non-public email addresses appear in any question or answer - contacts are referred to by
   role or service name instead.

---

# PART 1 - Derbyshire Healthcare trust policies and SOPs

> These come from the trust's own policy library. The source line names the actual policy, so
> staff can go and read it. Check the review date before treating any answer as current.

## Trust Policy - Seclusion & Segregation

**Q1. [Easy]** _Scenario: A patient becomes acutely disturbed and the team agree that seclusion is now the only safe option._ Under the Trust seclusion policy, who may authorise the implementation of seclusion?
- **A. The nurse in charge, a psychiatrist or an approved clinician [correct]**
- B. The bleep holder or the on-call manager
- C. Any registered nurse on shift
- D. Only the patient's Responsible Clinician (RC)
_Why:_ The policy states it must be either the nurse in charge, a psychiatrist or an approved clinician that authorises seclusion, and that person must have seen the patient immediately prior to it commencing.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Seclusion and Long-Term Segregation, Psychiatric Emergency Policy and Procedure (Nov 2024, issue 9, review Nov 2027 (version control amended 18 Apr 2026))

**Q2. [Easy]** How often must nursing reviews take place during an episode of seclusion under Trust policy?
- A. At least every hour
- B. At least twice in every 24 hours
- C. At least every four hours
- **D. At least every two hours [correct]**
_Why:_ The Trust policy and the seclusion timetable appendix both set nursing reviews at least every two hours, and they stay two hourly even after the first Multidisciplinary Team (MDT) review.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Seclusion and Long-Term Segregation, Psychiatric Emergency Policy and Procedure (Nov 2024, issue 9, review Nov 2027 (version control amended 18 Apr 2026))

**Q3. [Easy]** _Scenario: Seclusion commences at 14:00 and the patient's consultant was not involved in the decision._ By when must the initial medical review take place under Trust policy?
- **A. Within the first hour of seclusion commencing [correct]**
- B. Within two hours of seclusion commencing
- C. Within four hours of seclusion commencing
- D. At the next scheduled ward round
_Why:_ Where the consultant was not involved in the decision to seclude, the policy requires a face to face initial medical review within the first hour, recorded on the SystmOne 'Seclusion Review' document.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Seclusion and Long-Term Segregation, Psychiatric Emergency Policy and Procedure (Nov 2024, issue 9, review Nov 2027 (version control amended 18 Apr 2026))

**Q4. [Easy]** _Scenario: You have been allocated to observe a patient who has just gone into seclusion._ How often must the observing staff member document the patient's behaviour under Trust policy?
- A. Every 5 minutes
- B. Hourly
- C. Every 30 minutes
- **D. A minimum of every 15 minutes [correct]**
_Why:_ The policy requires continuous observation with a record made a minimum of every 15 minutes in the 'Seclusion Case Notes' section of SystmOne, including food and fluid intake, respiratory rate and ACVPU on the relevant charts.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Seclusion and Long-Term Segregation, Psychiatric Emergency Policy and Procedure (Nov 2024, issue 9, review Nov 2027 (version control amended 18 Apr 2026))

**Q5. [Easy]** Who normally takes the seclusion co-ordinator role at this Trust?
- **A. The nurse in charge of the shift, unless the role is allocated to someone else [correct]**
- B. The patient's named nurse
- C. The bleep holder
- D. The ward manager, who holds the role for the whole episode
_Why:_ The policy says the seclusion co-ordinator would be the nurse in charge on shift unless specifically nominated to another member of staff, and the role changes on a shift by shift basis.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Seclusion and Long-Term Segregation, Psychiatric Emergency Policy and Procedure (Nov 2024, issue 9, review Nov 2027 (version control amended 18 Apr 2026))

**Q6. [Easy]** When must the first Multidisciplinary Team (MDT) review of a seclusion episode happen, and how often after that?
- A. Within 12 hours, then every 12 hours
- B. Within 48 hours, then twice weekly
- **C. As soon as possible within 24 hours, then once in every 24 hours [correct]**
- D. At the next working day ward round, then weekly
_Why:_ The policy says the MDT review should take place as soon as possible within 24 hours of seclusion commencing, and then once in every 24 hours.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Seclusion and Long-Term Segregation, Psychiatric Emergency Policy and Procedure (Nov 2024, issue 9, review Nov 2027 (version control amended 18 Apr 2026))

**Q7. [Easy]** Which of these does the Trust policy state for a patient who is in seclusion?
- **A. They must never be deprived of their clothing and must always be able to see a clock [correct]**
- B. Clothing may be removed at the discretion of the nurse in charge
- C. A clock is only required in designated seclusion suites
- D. Reading material is prohibited in all cases
_Why:_ The policy states a patient in seclusion must never be deprived of their clothing, and that they should be able to always have sight of a clock, with access to sensory resources, radio or reading material where clinically appropriate and safe.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Seclusion and Long-Term Segregation, Psychiatric Emergency Policy and Procedure (Nov 2024, issue 9, review Nov 2027 (version control amended 18 Apr 2026))

**Q8. [Easy]** _Scenario: Seclusion has just commenced on your ward and you are completing the paperwork._ What level of DATIX does the Trust policy require to be completed when a seclusion episode starts?
- A. A Low DATIX
- B. No DATIX unless there was an injury
- C. A Major DATIX
- **D. A Moderate DATIX [correct]**
_Why:_ Under ward level monitoring the policy states that when a seclusion episode is commenced a Moderate DATIX will be completed as soon as possible, documenting which patient has required seclusion and why. Long-term segregation, by contrast, is recorded as Major.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Seclusion and Long-Term Segregation, Psychiatric Emergency Policy and Procedure (Nov 2024, issue 9, review Nov 2027 (version control amended 18 Apr 2026))

**Q9. [Easy]** What does the current Trust policy say about the door hatches on seclusion room doors?
- A. They may be used for passing food and drink only
- B. They may be used by two staff working together
- C. They may be used for medication administration
- **D. They must not be used, because of their proximity to the door handle [correct]**
_Why:_ A July 2025 amendment states door hatches must not be used due to their proximity to the door handle, and that each hatch carries a sign reading 'Warning, Hatch not in use'.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Seclusion and Long-Term Segregation, Psychiatric Emergency Policy and Procedure (Nov 2024, issue 9, review Nov 2027 (version control amended 18 Apr 2026))

**Q10. [Easy]** _Scenario: A patient is about to be placed in seclusion and staff have safely completed a search._ Where must the search and any property removed be recorded under Trust policy?
- **A. In the Seclusion Care Pathway, with removed property logged and held in safekeeping on the ward [correct]**
- B. In the ward property book only
- C. In a DATIX report only
- D. On the whiteboard outside the seclusion room
_Why:_ The policy requires the patient to be searched before seclusion starts, recorded within the 'Seclusion Care Pathway', with any property removed logged on that document and placed in safekeeping on the ward.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Seclusion and Long-Term Segregation, Psychiatric Emergency Policy and Procedure (Nov 2024, issue 9, review Nov 2027 (version control amended 18 Apr 2026))

**Q11. [Easy]** _Scenario: You are working through the Section 136 suite grab bag checklist at the start of your shift._ According to the Trust checklist, when should the oxygen cylinder be replaced?
- A. If it is less than half full
- B. Monthly, regardless of contents
- C. Only when it is empty
- **D. If it is less than three quarters full [correct]**
_Why:_ The Section 136 grab bag checklist instructs staff to replace the oxygen cylinder if it is less than three quarters full. The checklist is laid out with a column for each day of the week, so it is a daily check.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Section 136 Suite Emergency Equipment Grab Bag Checklist (V5, 08 April 2025)

**Q12. [Medium]** _Scenario: Two hours into a seclusion episode the nursing review is due. You were part of the team that took the decision to seclude._ What does the Trust policy require of the staff carrying out the nursing review?
- A. One registered nurse, who may be anyone on shift
- B. Two registered nurses, both independent of the ward
- C. One registered nurse and one healthcare assistant
- **D. Two registered nurses, one of whom was not involved in the decision to seclude [correct]**
_Why:_ The policy states the nursing review must include two registered nurses, one of whom was not involved in the decision to seclude.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Seclusion and Long-Term Segregation, Psychiatric Emergency Policy and Procedure (Nov 2024, issue 9, review Nov 2027 (version control amended 18 Apr 2026))

**Q13. [Medium]** After the initial medical review, how often are medical reviews required until the patient has their first Multidisciplinary Team (MDT) review?
- A. Every two hours
- B. Twice in 24 hours
- C. Every six hours
- **D. Every four hours [correct]**
_Why:_ The policy requires four hourly medical reviews after the initial medical review until the first MDT review. Only after that initial MDT review do medical reviews drop to at least twice in every 24 hours.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Seclusion and Long-Term Segregation, Psychiatric Emergency Policy and Procedure (Nov 2024, issue 9, review Nov 2027 (version control amended 18 Apr 2026))

**Q14. [Medium]** _Scenario: A patient has been in seclusion since 22:00 and the 24 hour Multidisciplinary Team (MDT) review falls in the night._ Who must the out of hours MDT review consist of under Trust policy?
- A. Two registered nurses and the seclusion co-ordinator
- B. The bleep holder alone
- **C. A nursing member of the leadership team or the bleep holder, and the on-call consultant or nominated deputy [correct]**
- D. The ward manager and the patient's Responsible Clinician (RC)
_Why:_ Out of hours the policy requires the MDT review to consist of a nursing member of the leadership team, or the bleep holder, together with the on-call consultant or their nominated deputy.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Seclusion and Long-Term Segregation, Psychiatric Emergency Policy and Procedure (Nov 2024, issue 9, review Nov 2027 (version control amended 18 Apr 2026))

**Q15. [Medium]** _Scenario: No designated seclusion room is available, so a patient meeting the criteria for seclusion is secluded in a quiet room._ Who must be notified under Trust policy?
- **A. The Matron, the Area Service Manager and the Head of Nursing [correct]**
- B. The bleep holder only
- C. The Care Quality Commission within 24 hours
- D. The Executive Director of Nursing only
_Why:_ For seclusion outside a designated seclusion room, the policy requires the Matron, Area Service Manager and Head of Nursing to be notified, the use of a non-designated room to be clearly documented on the DATIX, and the full seclusion pathway to be followed.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Seclusion and Long-Term Segregation, Psychiatric Emergency Policy and Procedure (Nov 2024, issue 9, review Nov 2027 (version control amended 18 Apr 2026))

**Q16. [Medium]** _Scenario: At a two hourly nursing review the patient is calm and settled and the nurses feel seclusion could safely end._ Can that nursing review end the seclusion under Trust policy?
- A. No, only a Multidisciplinary Team (MDT) or independent review can end seclusion
- B. Yes, but only if the patient has been secluded for less than four hours
- **C. Yes, following a discussion with the Responsible Clinician (RC) or duty doctor, in person or by telephone [correct]**
- D. No, nursing reviews can only recommend a reduction in restrictions
_Why:_ The policy lists a nurses review as one of the four routes to ending seclusion, provided there has been a discussion with the Responsible Clinician or duty doctor, which can be in person or on the telephone.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Seclusion and Long-Term Segregation, Psychiatric Emergency Policy and Procedure (Nov 2024, issue 9, review Nov 2027 (version control amended 18 Apr 2026))

**Q17. [Medium]** _Scenario: As part of a graded plan, the seclusion door is opened for a planned period so the patient can use the secure courtyard._ What does the Trust policy say about this?
- A. It ends the seclusion episode and a new episode must be opened on return
- **B. It does not constitute an end to seclusion [correct]**
- C. It converts the episode to long-term segregation
- D. It pauses the review clock until the patient returns
_Why:_ The policy notes explicitly that opening the door for planned periods, for example access to the secure courtyard, does not constitute an end to seclusion. This kind of flexibility is described as a way of evaluating the patient under less restriction without terminating the episode.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Seclusion and Long-Term Segregation, Psychiatric Emergency Policy and Procedure (Nov 2024, issue 9, review Nov 2027 (version control amended 18 Apr 2026))

**Q18. [Medium]** _Scenario: The ward is short staffed and a student nurse on placement offers to sit on the seclusion observation._ How does the Trust policy define who can carry out seclusion observations?
- A. Any member of the nursing team, including students under supervision
- B. Registered nurses only
- **C. A suitably skilled professional who has completed the observation skills booklet and is compliant on the Electronic Staff Record (ESR); student nurses and student Allied Health Professionals cannot do it [correct]**
- D. Any staff member who has completed Positive and Safe training
_Why:_ The policy defines a suitably skilled professional as someone who has completed the observation skills booklet and is compliant on ESR, and states that student nurses and student AHPs cannot undertake observations.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Seclusion and Long-Term Segregation, Psychiatric Emergency Policy and Procedure (Nov 2024, issue 9, review Nov 2027 (version control amended 18 Apr 2026))

**Q19. [Medium]** _Scenario: A four hourly medical review was missed overnight because no doctor was available._ What does the Trust policy require when a seclusion review is not completed as set out in the process?
- A. Nothing, provided the next review happens on time
- **B. The rationale must be documented in the Electronic Patient Record (EPR) and a DATIX completed [correct]**
- C. The seclusion episode must be ended immediately
- D. The seclusion co-ordinator must inform the Care Quality Commission
_Why:_ The policy states at the start of the review section that if any reviews are not completed as per the process, the rationale must be documented in EPR and a DATIX completed. It also requires a DATIX by the seclusion co-ordinator if a doctor does not arrive for the initial medical review.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Seclusion and Long-Term Segregation, Psychiatric Emergency Policy and Procedure (Nov 2024, issue 9, review Nov 2027 (version control amended 18 Apr 2026))

**Q20. [Medium]** _Scenario: A Multidisciplinary Team (MDT) is considering whether a patient with a sustained risk of harm to others should be placed on long-term segregation (LTS)._ What does the Trust policy say about providing LTS at this Trust?
- A. Every inpatient unit has an LTS suite available
- B. LTS can be started by the ward Multidisciplinary Team alone if the risk is sustained
- **C. The Trust does not have the clinical environment for LTS, so it may only be considered in extenuating circumstances and only after written confirmation from the Director of Nursing, AHPs and Patient Experience [correct]**
- D. LTS is prohibited outright at this Trust with no exceptions
_Why:_ The policy states DHCFT does not have the clinical environment necessary to care for people in LTS, that it should only be considered in extenuating circumstances, and that it can only be instigated following written confirmation from the Director of Nursing, AHPs and Patient Experience.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Seclusion and Long-Term Segregation, Psychiatric Emergency Policy and Procedure (Nov 2024, issue 9, review Nov 2027 (version control amended 18 Apr 2026))

**Q21. [Medium]** _Scenario: Long-term segregation (LTS) has just been agreed for a patient._ Who is responsible for the LTS care plan and within what timeframe must it be completed?
- A. The Responsible Clinician (RC), within 72 hours
- **B. The ward manager, within 24 hours of LTS commencing [correct]**
- C. The named nurse, by the end of the first week
- D. The Head of Nursing, within 48 hours
_Why:_ The policy makes the ward manager responsible for ensuring an LTS care plan is completed within 24 hours of LTS commencing, focused on what needs to be achieved for LTS to be terminated.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Seclusion and Long-Term Segregation, Psychiatric Emergency Policy and Procedure (Nov 2024, issue 9, review Nov 2027 (version control amended 18 Apr 2026))

**Q22. [Medium]** Under the Trust's seclusion monitoring arrangements, who is responsible for ensuring the seclusion suite is fit for use daily?
- A. The ward manager
- **B. The bleep holder [correct]**
- C. The Positive and Safe team
- D. The estates department
_Why:_ The ward level monitoring section states that the bleep holder will ensure the seclusion suite is fit for use daily, and that the suite is checked a minimum of once per day and cleaned and made ready as soon as possible after an episode ends.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Seclusion and Long-Term Segregation, Psychiatric Emergency Policy and Procedure (Nov 2024, issue 9, review Nov 2027 (version control amended 18 Apr 2026))

**Q23. [Hard]** _Scenario: The eight hour independent review is being arranged._ Who must make up the independent Multidisciplinary Team (MDT) review under Trust policy?
- A. Any three members of the ward team not on duty at the time of the incident
- B. The Responsible Clinician (RC), the ward manager and the seclusion co-ordinator
- C. A Matron, a Head of Nursing and a commissioner representative
- **D. An approved clinician or consultant psychiatrist (or nominated deputy), a nurse, and an Independent Mental Health Advocate (IMHA) where the patient has one, none of whom were involved in the decision to seclude [correct]**
_Why:_ The policy specifies the independent review membership as an approved clinician or consultant psychiatrist (or nominated deputy), a nurse, and an IMHA in cases where the patient has one, all of whom must not have been involved in the initial decision to seclude.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Seclusion and Long-Term Segregation, Psychiatric Emergency Policy and Procedure (Nov 2024, issue 9, review Nov 2027 (version control amended 18 Apr 2026))

**Q24. [Hard]** _Scenario: A seclusion episode has now run beyond seven days._ What does Trust policy require for prolonged seclusion of over seven days?
- **A. A risk strategy meeting organised by the Responsible Clinician (RC) or nominated deputy, followed by a weekly independent Multidisciplinary Team (MDT) review [correct]**
- B. Automatic conversion to long-term segregation
- C. A referral to the Care Quality Commission
- D. Daily independent reviews until seclusion ends
_Why:_ The policy states that in the event of prolonged seclusion over 7 days a risk strategy meeting should be organised by the RC or their nominated deputy, after which an independent MDT reviews the patient once a week to identify ways to reduce restrictions or end seclusion.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Seclusion and Long-Term Segregation, Psychiatric Emergency Policy and Procedure (Nov 2024, issue 9, review Nov 2027 (version control amended 18 Apr 2026))

**Q25. [Hard]** _Scenario: It is 03:00, a four hourly medical review is due and the patient is asleep. The doctor and seclusion co-ordinator agree it would not be appropriate to wake them._ The policy allows the doctor's attendance to be replaced by a telephone call with the seclusion co-ordinator. What limit does it place on this?
- A. It can only be used once during the whole episode
- **B. No more than two consecutive four hourly medical reviews may be undertaken in this way [correct]**
- C. It can be used for any review overnight without limit
- D. It is only permitted at weekends and on bank holidays
_Why:_ The policy permits a telephone call with the seclusion co-ordinator to replace the doctor's attendance for a night time four hourly medical review when the patient is asleep, recorded on the SystmOne 'Seclusion Review' document, but no more than two consecutive reviews may be done this way.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Seclusion and Long-Term Segregation, Psychiatric Emergency Policy and Procedure (Nov 2024, issue 9, review Nov 2027 (version control amended 18 Apr 2026))

**Q26. [Hard]** _Scenario: A patient is being managed under the long-term segregation (LTS) pathway._ Which set of reviews matches the Trust policy for LTS?
- A. Two hourly nursing reviews, four hourly medical reviews and a weekly Multidisciplinary Team (MDT) review
- **B. Hourly documentation by observing staff, an approved clinician review every 24 hours, a weekly MDT review including an Independent Mental Health Advocate (IMHA) or Independent Mental Capacity Advocate (IMCA) if the patient has one, and four weekly reviews by a professional not involved with the case [correct]**
- C. Daily approved clinician review and a monthly MDT review only
- D. 15 minute observation records and a fortnightly independent review
_Why:_ The LTS section sets out hourly documentation of the patient's condition by observing staff, physical health monitoring as per care plan, an approved clinician review every 24 hours, a weekly MDT review including an IMHA or IMCA if the patient has one, and four weekly reviews by a professional not involved with the case.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Seclusion and Long-Term Segregation, Psychiatric Emergency Policy and Procedure (Nov 2024, issue 9, review Nov 2027 (version control amended 18 Apr 2026))

**Q27. [Hard]** _Scenario: Long-term segregation (LTS) for a patient has now continued for more than three months._ What additional review does Trust policy require, and who arranges it?
- A. A Care Quality Commission inspection, arranged by the Trust board
- B. An extra internal Multidisciplinary Team (MDT) review, arranged by the Responsible Clinician (RC)
- **C. External three monthly reviews arranged by the ward manager, including a professional from outside the Trust, an Independent Mental Health Advocate (IMHA) where possible and a commissioner where appropriate [correct]**
- D. A second opinion appointed doctor visit, arranged by the Mental Health Act office
_Why:_ Where LTS continues for more than 3 months the policy requires external three monthly reviews to be arranged by the ward manager, including a professional from outside the Trust, an IMHA where possible and a commissioner where appropriate.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Seclusion and Long-Term Segregation, Psychiatric Emergency Policy and Procedure (Nov 2024, issue 9, review Nov 2027 (version control amended 18 Apr 2026))

**Q28. [Hard]** How must long-term segregation (LTS) be recorded on DATIX under Trust policy?
- A. As a Moderate incident, the same as seclusion
- **B. As a Major incident, categorised as a restrictive practice [correct]**
- C. As a Low incident for information only
- D. It is not recorded on DATIX, only in the Electronic Patient Record
_Why:_ The LTS record keeping section states the practice must be recorded via DATIX as Major and as a Restrictive Practice. This is a step up from the Moderate DATIX required at the start of a seclusion episode.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Seclusion and Long-Term Segregation, Psychiatric Emergency Policy and Procedure (Nov 2024, issue 9, review Nov 2027 (version control amended 18 Apr 2026))

**Q29. [Hard]** _Scenario: A patient in seclusion at the Radbourne Unit needs intramuscular lorazepam._ How does the June 2025 Trust procedure say this is managed?
- A. It is kept in the ambient cupboard in the seclusion suite with the other emergency medication
- B. It is collected from the on-site pharmacy by the bleep holder
- **C. Because it needs refrigeration, it is prepared on the patient's ward and transported to the seclusion suite in a green EVO bag provided by Pharmacy [correct]**
- D. It is not permitted in seclusion, so an oral alternative must be used
_Why:_ The June 2025 procedure explains that intramuscular lorazepam needs refrigeration, so it is prepared on the patient's ward and transported to the seclusion suite in a green EVO bag provided by Pharmacy. Other stock injectables and emergency medicines sit in an ambient cupboard in the seclusion suite accessed by a key safe.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Seclusion and Long-Term Segregation, Psychiatric Emergency Policy and Procedure (Nov 2024, issue 9, review Nov 2027 (version control amended 18 Apr 2026))

**Q30. [Hard]** _Scenario: For a second time, the team judge it is not safe to enter the seclusion room to carry out a review._ What does Trust policy require next?
- **A. Escalate to the bleep holder, who determines the further escalation route such as the Area Service Manager or on-call manager, and consider completing a DATIX [correct]**
- B. Skip the review and record it as not required
- C. End the seclusion immediately
- D. Wait until the next scheduled review and try again
_Why:_ The policy says reviews should be carried out in the seclusion room where safe and therapeutic, and if it is deemed not safe to enter on more than one occasion the team must escalate to the bleep holder, who determines the further escalation route, with a DATIX also to be considered.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Seclusion and Long-Term Segregation, Psychiatric Emergency Policy and Procedure (Nov 2024, issue 9, review Nov 2027 (version control amended 18 Apr 2026))

**Q31. [Hard]** _Scenario: The team are considering tear-proof clothing for a patient in seclusion with a very high risk of using shredded clothing to self-harm._ What does the Trust policy require before this is used, and where is it recorded?
- A. The nurse in charge can decide, recorded in the case notes
- **B. An individualised Multidisciplinary Team (MDT) risk assessment, with the use recorded in the 'Seclusion Support Plan' [correct]**
- C. Approval from the Head of Nursing, recorded on DATIX
- D. It is a standard precaution for all patients in seclusion so no separate assessment is needed
_Why:_ The policy requires an individualised risk assessment by the MDT before tear-proof clothing is used, records that use in the 'Seclusion Support Plan', and states it must never be a first line response, never substitute for enhanced observation, and never be a blanket rule within a service.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Seclusion and Long-Term Segregation, Psychiatric Emergency Policy and Procedure (Nov 2024, issue 9, review Nov 2027 (version control amended 18 Apr 2026))

**Q32. [Hard]** _Scenario: The first 24 hour Multidisciplinary Team (MDT) review has taken place and seclusion is continuing._ What is the medical review requirement from that point onwards under Trust policy?
- A. Medical reviews continue every four hours
- B. Medical reviews are only required if the nursing team request one
- **C. Medical reviews must take place at least twice in every 24 hours [correct]**
- D. Medical reviews reduce to once every 24 hours alongside the MDT review
_Why:_ The policy states medical reviews must take place at least twice every 24 hours after the initial MDT review, while nursing reviews stay two hourly and MDT reviews continue at least once every 24 hours.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Seclusion and Long-Term Segregation, Psychiatric Emergency Policy and Procedure (Nov 2024, issue 9, review Nov 2027 (version control amended 18 Apr 2026))

## Trust Policy - Restrictive Practice

**Q33. [Easy]** _Scenario: A patient asks why their mobile phone charger was taken off them at admission, saying "it's just the ward rule, isn't it?"_ Under the trust's Blanket Restrictions Policy, which of these should NOT normally be applied as a blanket restriction across a ward?
- A. Ignition sources such as lighters and matches
- B. Weapons, including knives and firearms
- **C. Access to (or banning) mobile phones and chargers [correct]**
- D. Alcohol on trust premises
_Why:_ The policy lists access to mobile phones and chargers as something that should not form part of a blanket restriction - any restriction must be individually justified and documented. The other three are authorised trust-wide blanket restrictions.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Blanket Restrictions Policy and Procedure (Nov 2025, issue 3, review Nov 2028 (minor amendments Feb and Mar 2026))

**Q34. [Easy]** On the trust's restricted and prohibited items list for working age adult acute inpatient services, which of these is a banned item that is not allowed on the ward at all?
- **A. Coat hangers [correct]**
- B. Nail clippers
- C. Aerosols
- D. Essential oil
_Why:_ Coat hangers appear on the banned/prohibited list alongside plastic bags, tobacco, chewing gum and vapes bought outside the hospital. Nail clippers, aerosols and essential oils are restricted items, allowed after a risk assessment and stored securely.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Blanket Restrictions Policy and Procedure (Nov 2025, issue 3, review Nov 2028 (minor amendments Feb and Mar 2026))

**Q35. [Medium]** The trust's restricted items list covers items that can be accessed but only after a risk assessment and which must be stored in a secure place. Which group of items does the policy give as examples?
- A. Illicit drugs, alcohol and New Psychoactive Substances
- **B. Glass items such as mirrors and bottles, scissors, razors, aerosols and metal cans [correct]**
- C. Large amounts of money, expensive jewellery and expensive clothing
- D. Tobacco, chewing gum and vapes purchased outside the hospital
_Why:_ The appendix splits items into banned, restricted and advisory. Restricted items (risk assessed before use, stored securely) include glass items, scissors, nail clippers and files, razors, aerosols, essential oil and metal cans. Money and jewellery sit under advisory, the rest are banned outright.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Blanket Restrictions Policy and Procedure (Nov 2025, issue 3, review Nov 2028 (minor amendments Feb and Mar 2026))

**Q36. [Hard]** _Scenario: A patient's relative wants to bring in cash so the patient can buy things from the shop._ What does the trust's advisory section of the restricted and prohibited items list say about money on the ward?
- **A. No more than 20 pounds should be brought or kept on the ward [correct]**
- B. No more than 50 pounds should be brought or kept on the ward
- C. No cash at all is permitted, only a bank card
- D. There is no advisory limit, money is always held in the ward safe
_Why:_ The advisory part of the list (items advised not to be brought or kept on the ward) names large amounts of money, specified as no more than 20 pounds, along with expensive jewellery, watches, items and clothing.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Blanket Restrictions Policy and Procedure (Nov 2025, issue 3, review Nov 2028 (minor amendments Feb and Mar 2026))

**Q37. [Medium]** Who does the Blanket Restrictions Policy make responsible for keeping a register of the blanket restrictions in place, so their extent is transparent and can be reviewed?
- A. The Responsible Clinician
- B. The Matron
- **C. The Ward Manager [correct]**
- D. The Care Group Deputy Operational Manager
_Why:_ The policy states the Ward Manager should keep a register of blanket restrictions in place so that the extent of any blanket restrictions is transparent and can be regularly reviewed.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Blanket Restrictions Policy and Procedure (Nov 2025, issue 3, review Nov 2028 (minor amendments Feb and Mar 2026))

**Q38. [Medium]** _Scenario: The night shift decides a new restriction is needed across the whole ward to keep everyone safe._ Under the trust policy, how far must the decision to implement a blanket restriction on a ward be escalated as a minimum?
- **A. Through normal line management arrangements at least to the level of the Ward Manager [correct]**
- B. To the Executive Director of Nursing on the same shift
- C. To the Responsible Clinician only
- D. No escalation is needed if the restriction lasts under 24 hours
_Why:_ The policy says the decision should be escalated through normal line management arrangements at least to the level of the Ward Manager, and that if the Ward Manager judges it needs to go further an exception report can be made to the Care Group Deputy Operational Manager or Matron.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Blanket Restrictions Policy and Procedure (Nov 2025, issue 3, review Nov 2028 (minor amendments Feb and Mar 2026))

**Q39. [Hard]** How often must a Ward Manager ensure blanket restrictions are discussed as part of the ward's clinical routine?
- A. Weekly, at the Multidisciplinary Team (MDT) meeting
- **B. Daily, as part of the huddle or handover [correct]**
- C. Monthly, at the clinical governance meeting
- D. Every time a new patient is admitted
_Why:_ The local accountability section requires Ward Managers to ensure blanket restrictions are discussed on a daily basis as part of the ward clinical routine (huddle or handover), with agreed review dates and processes for both ward and individual restrictions.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Blanket Restrictions Policy and Procedure (Nov 2025, issue 3, review Nov 2028 (minor amendments Feb and Mar 2026))

**Q40. [Hard]** _Scenario: A restriction put in place on a ward looks like it will need to stay in place with no end date in sight._ If a ward-level blanket restriction needs to be in operation for an indefinite period, what does the trust policy require?
- A. It is signed off by the nurse in charge and reviewed at the next handover
- B. It is automatically lawful once entered on Datix
- **C. It is agreed with the Hospital Managers and discussed at the Reducing Restrictive Practice Group for oversight and governance [correct]**
- D. It is referred to the Care Quality Commission for approval
_Why:_ The policy says a restriction over and above those authorised trust-wide should be for the shortest reasonable time, and if it needs to run indefinitely it must be agreed with the Hospital Managers and discussed at the Reducing Restrictive Practice Group. It must then be clearly communicated to service users and visitors.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Blanket Restrictions Policy and Procedure (Nov 2025, issue 3, review Nov 2028 (minor amendments Feb and Mar 2026))

**Q41. [Hard]** _Scenario: A patient on the Carsington Unit asks why the quiet room on the bedroom corridor is locked when the other rooms are not._ What reason does the trust's Blanket Restrictions Policy give for the quiet rooms on the Carsington Unit and Derwent Unit being locked?
- A. The rooms are used for storage overnight
- B. The rooms are only for patients on enhanced observations
- **C. Their doors are not fitted with a door alarm system, unlike all other doors in that area, so patients access them when escorted by staff [correct]**
- D. The rooms are reserved for de-escalation following a physical intervention
_Why:_ A February 2026 amendment added these quiet rooms to the authorised trust-wide list. The rationale given is that unlike every other door in the bedroom corridor these doors have no door alarm system, so they are locked and patients access them when escorted by staff.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Blanket Restrictions Policy and Procedure (Nov 2025, issue 3, review Nov 2028 (minor amendments Feb and Mar 2026))

**Q42. [Easy]** The Locking of Doors on Open Wards policy sets minimum standards for practice. What must every ward area display?
- **A. Clear information on either side of the ward doors telling patients and visitors how to leave and access the ward [correct]**
- B. A laminated copy of the full policy at the nurses' station
- C. A list of which patients are detained and which are informal
- D. The name of the nurse holding the door key that shift
_Why:_ The minimum standards require clear information displayed on both sides of the ward doors explaining how patients and visitors can leave and access the ward. An example sign is provided in the policy appendix to print and laminate.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Locking of Doors on Open Wards All Units Policy and Procedure (December 2025, issue 5, review December 2028)

**Q43. [Easy]** _Scenario: You are admitting an informal patient to a locked acute ward._ According to the trust policy, what must the information given to an informal patient about access and exit include?
- A. A written undertaking not to leave the ward without permission
- **B. Details of how they can discharge themselves from the hospital [correct]**
- C. The ward's staffing numbers for that shift
- D. A copy of the door code so they can leave independently
_Why:_ The policy is explicit that information provided to informal patients must include details of how they can discharge themselves from hospital, discussed as part of a conversation about their care planned needs.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Locking of Doors on Open Wards All Units Policy and Procedure (December 2025, issue 5, review December 2028)

**Q44. [Easy]** Where does the Locking of Doors policy say the rationale for locking the ward doors should be discussed and reviewed by staff?
- A. Only at the annual policy update meeting
- B. Only in the Multidisciplinary Team (MDT) ward round
- C. At the monthly team meeting and in the ward newsletter
- **D. At handover, as well as during supervision [correct]**
_Why:_ The policy requires all staff to be clear about the reasons and purpose for locking the doors, with the rationale discussed and reviewed at handover and during supervision, so staff feel confident explaining the clinical reasons to patients.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Locking of Doors on Open Wards All Units Policy and Procedure (December 2025, issue 5, review December 2028)

**Q45. [Medium]** Who carries out the annual audit of locked door practice at this trust?
- A. The Mental Health Act office
- **B. The Positive and Safe Team [correct]**
- C. The Care Quality Commission
- D. Each ward manager for their own ward
_Why:_ The audit and review section of the policy states that an annual audit of locked door practice will be undertaken by the Positive and Safe Team.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Locking of Doors on Open Wards All Units Policy and Procedure (December 2025, issue 5, review December 2028)

**Q46. [Medium]** The Locking of Doors policy names one unit whose access and exit arrangements sit outside the standard trust approach. Which unit, and why?
- A. The Radbourne Unit, because it takes emergency admissions
- B. The Carsington Unit, because of its quiet room arrangements
- C. The Derwent Unit, because of its dementia care beds
- **D. The Kedleston Unit, because it is a low secure unit governed by NHS England and Ministry of Justice requirements [correct]**
_Why:_ The external governance section states that the Kedleston Unit is a low secure unit and its access and exit arrangements are governed by the requirements of NHS England and the Ministry of Justice.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Locking of Doors on Open Wards All Units Policy and Procedure (December 2025, issue 5, review December 2028)

**Q47. [Hard]** _Scenario: An informal patient with capacity wants to leave the ward. You do not think it is clinically safe, persuasion has not worked, and a doctor has been asked to attend for a section 5(2) assessment._ What does the trust policy say the nurse should do about the delay in the doctor arriving?
- A. Wait for the doctor however long it takes, using persuasion only
- B. Use common law to hold the patient for up to two hours
- **C. Consider using section 5(4) of the Mental Health Act 1983 if there is a delay of 15 minutes or more [correct]**
- D. Call the police to detain the patient under section 136
_Why:_ The policy states that if there is a delay of 15 minutes or more in the doctor coming to do the assessment, the nurse should consider the use of section 5(4). It also warns that section 5 must never be used as a threat to deter a patient from trying to leave.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Locking of Doors on Open Wards All Units Policy and Procedure (December 2025, issue 5, review December 2028)

**Q48. [Hard]** _Scenario: A patient subject to a Community Treatment Order (CTO) has been admitted informally, not recalled. They now want to leave and staff are concerned._ What does the trust policy say about holding this patient?
- A. Section 5 holding powers can be used as normal because they are subject to a CTO
- **B. They cannot be held under section 5 while informal, so they would need to be held under common law until the Responsible Clinician completes the CTO3 recall papers [correct]**
- C. They can be held for 72 hours by the nurse in charge without any further paperwork
- D. A Deprivation of Liberty Safeguards (DoLS) urgent authorisation must be granted first
_Why:_ The policy states that a patient admitted informally while still subject to a CTO cannot be made subject to the section 5 holding powers, and would need to be held under common law until the Responsible Clinician completes the CTO3 recall papers. It adds that it may be necessary to lock the ward doors during this time, and this should be explained and documented.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Locking of Doors on Open Wards All Units Policy and Procedure (December 2025, issue 5, review December 2028)

**Q49. [Hard]** The trust reviewed the blanket restriction of locking ward entry doors and the front doors of the inpatient units. How often is that decision reviewed under the current policy?
- **A. As a minimum every three years in line with the policy update, and also discussed as part of weekly inpatient committee meetings [correct]**
- B. Every six months by the Mental Health Act Committee only
- C. Annually by each ward manager
- D. Only when a complaint about the locked door is received
_Why:_ The version control note records that following review the locked door restriction was agreed to be sustained, will be reviewed as a minimum every three years in line with this policy update, and will also be discussed in line with all blanket restrictions as part of weekly inpatient committee meetings.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Locking of Doors on Open Wards All Units Policy and Procedure (December 2025, issue 5, review December 2028)

**Q50. [Easy]** _Scenario: A colleague suggests fastening a tray across a chair to stop a patient with dementia getting up and falling._ What does the trust's Prevention and Management of Violence and Aggression (Use of Force) Policy say about mechanical restraint?
- A. It is permitted on older adult wards with a best interest decision
- B. It is permitted if the Responsible Clinician authorises it in writing
- **C. The use of mechanical restraint is not permitted within the Trust [correct]**
- D. It is permitted for up to two hours with hourly nursing review
_Why:_ The policy states plainly that the use of mechanical restraint is not permitted within the Trust, and gives fastening trays to chairs, tying a patient to an object, facing chairs to walls and hooking clothes over chairs as examples the Trust does not support.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Prevention and Management of Violence and Aggression (Use of Force) Policy (March 2026, issue 3, review March 2029)

**Q51. [Easy]** After every incident of violence and aggression, where must staff create a record?
- **A. On SystmOne and on Datix, as soon as practicable [correct]**
- B. In the ward diary and the handover sheet
- C. On Datix only, unless someone was injured
- D. In the patient's paper notes only
_Why:_ The recording and reporting section requires a record to be created as soon as practicable on SystmOne and on Datix after every incident, and says records must be factual, timed, dated and attributable.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Prevention and Management of Violence and Aggression (Use of Force) Policy (March 2026, issue 3, review March 2029)

**Q52. [Easy]** _Scenario: Police officers attend the ward on duty to assist staff with a violent incident._ What does the trust policy say about body-worn cameras in this situation?
- A. Staff must ask the officers to switch cameras off to protect confidentiality
- **B. Officers must wear and operate body-worn cameras when reasonably practicable, and staff should inform the patient where practicable and record that a camera was used [correct]**
- C. Body-worn cameras are only used if the patient consents in writing
- D. The ward must download and store the footage in the patient record
_Why:_ The police involvement section states officers are required by law to wear and operate body-worn cameras when reasonably practicable. Staff should inform the patient and others present where practicable, support privacy and dignity, and record that the camera was used. Requests for footage are managed by the police.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Prevention and Management of Violence and Aggression (Use of Force) Policy (March 2026, issue 3, review March 2029)

**Q53. [Medium]** Under the Mental Health Units (Use of Force) Act 2018 a provider must appoint a 'responsible person'. Which post holds that role at this trust?
- A. The Chief Executive
- **B. The Associate Director of Physical Health and Quality [correct]**
- C. The Local Security Management Specialist (LSMS)
- D. The Mental Health Act Committee chair
_Why:_ The policy names the Associate Director of Physical Health and Quality as the appointed Responsible Person, accountable for publishing and maintaining the policy, patient information, training, and the recording, quality, analysis and governance of use of force data.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Prevention and Management of Violence and Aggression (Use of Force) Policy (March 2026, issue 3, review March 2029)

**Q54. [Medium]** _Scenario: A physical intervention is under way with several staff involved._ What does the trust policy say about the role of one specific team member during the intervention?
- A. One member records the timings on a stopwatch for the Datix report
- B. One member stands outside the room to keep other patients away
- **C. One member is responsible for protecting and supporting the head and neck, leads the team through the intervention, and ensures the airway and breathing are not compromised and vital signs are monitored [correct]**
- D. One member must be the patient's named nurse, whatever their training
_Why:_ The policy states that during physical intervention one team member should be responsible for protecting and supporting the head and neck where required, should lead the team through the process, and should ensure the airway and breathing are not compromised and vital signs are monitored.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Prevention and Management of Violence and Aggression (Use of Force) Policy (March 2026, issue 3, review March 2029)

**Q55. [Medium]** In addition to the Datix incident report, what extra record does the trust require for any incident that needed physical intervention?
- A. A paper restraint log kept in the ward office
- **B. A post incident physical intervention monitoring form completed via SystmOne [correct]**
- C. A safeguarding referral to the local authority
- D. An entry in the Mental Health Act statutory records
_Why:_ The managers' responsibilities section requires all incidents of violence and aggression to be reported on the electronic incident form (Datix), and states that any incident requiring physical intervention must also have a post incident physical intervention monitoring form completed via SystmOne.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Prevention and Management of Violence and Aggression (Use of Force) Policy (March 2026, issue 3, review March 2029)

**Q56. [Medium]** _Scenario: A visitor on the ward produces a knife during an argument._ What does the trust policy instruct staff to do when threats are made with a weapon?
- **A. Make no attempt to disarm the person, remove yourself and others, evacuate to a safe area, and call the police using the SBARD tool stating 'Weapons - police assistance is required' [correct]**
- B. Attempt to disarm the person using the taught breakaway techniques
- C. Contain the person in a side room until the on-call manager arrives
- D. Sound the personal alarm and wait for the response team before evacuating anyone
_Why:_ The policy is explicit that no attempt should be made to disarm the individual. The priority is removing yourself and others, keeping the situation contained, evacuating service users, visitors and staff to a safe area, and calling the police using the SBARD communication tool with the phrase 'Weapons - police assistance is required'.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Prevention and Management of Violence and Aggression (Use of Force) Policy (March 2026, issue 3, review March 2029)

**Q57. [Medium]** _Scenario: The police bring a patient to the unit. Staff are not sure whether the patient was restrained on the way in._ What does the trust policy require staff to do?
- A. Assume no restraint took place unless the police hand over paperwork saying otherwise
- **B. Ask whether the patient was physically or mechanically restrained, and if so or if unsure commence physical observations, record the discussion in the clinical record and complete a body map [correct]**
- C. Record it on Datix only and take observations at the next routine round
- D. Refuse admission until the police confirm in writing what happened
_Why:_ The policy requires staff to ask whether the patient has been physically or mechanically restrained by the police or external agency. If so, or if staff are unsure, physical observations should commence, the discussion must be recorded in the clinical record and a body map completed. Staff must also establish whether the patient has been searched.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Prevention and Management of Violence and Aggression (Use of Force) Policy (March 2026, issue 3, review March 2029)

**Q58. [Hard]** How does the trust's Positive and Safe training programme run for staff working on inpatient wards?
- A. A three day induction, then a half day refresher every two years
- **B. A five day Positive and Safe Teamwork induction on appointment, then a two day update package annually [correct]**
- C. A two day induction, then a five day update every three years
- D. A one day Breakaway package on appointment, repeated annually
_Why:_ The training section sets out a five day Positive and Safe Teamwork induction package when employed on inpatient wards and a two day update package annually after induction. The one day Breakaway package is the annual requirement for community staff and other patient facing staff such as facilities, estates and catering.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Prevention and Management of Violence and Aggression (Use of Force) Policy (March 2026, issue 3, review March 2029)

**Q59. [Hard]** _Scenario: A care hold agreed in a patient's care plan is used to help with personal care. Afterwards a small scratch is noticed on the patient's arm._ Under the trust's rules on care holds and 'negligible force', what follows?
- A. Nothing further is needed because the hold was already in the care plan
- B. The hold is recorded in the care plan review only, as scratches are not classed as injury
- **C. The intervention can no longer be counted as negligible force and the incident must be documented via Datix [correct]**
- D. The care plan must be suspended until a Best Interest Meeting is held
_Why:_ The policy states force can never be negligible if it causes an injury to a patient or staff member, including scratches, marks or bruising. The same applies if rapid tranquillisation was involved, the patient resisted beyond what was agreed, more staff were used than the care plan specifies, or the patient was left distressed outside their usual pattern. In those cases the incident must go on Datix.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Prevention and Management of Violence and Aggression (Use of Force) Policy (March 2026, issue 3, review March 2029)

**Q60. [Hard]** _Scenario: A patient who cannot comply needs blood taken, and the team is considering holding their arm to do it._ What does the trust policy require before clinical holding is used?
- **A. It must be planned, care planned, and a Best Interest Meeting must have taken place beforehand [correct]**
- B. It can be used at short notice as long as it is recorded on Datix afterwards
- C. It only needs the agreement of the two staff carrying it out
- D. It requires authorisation from the Mental Health Act office
_Why:_ The policy states that all clinical holding interventions are planned interventions and therefore must be care planned, with a Best Interest Meeting having taken place beforehand. Clinical holding is identified as restraint and must be recorded in line with the Mental Health Units (Use of Force) Act 2018, with post-incident review after each intervention.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Prevention and Management of Violence and Aggression (Use of Force) Policy (March 2026, issue 3, review March 2029)

**Q61. [Hard]** _Scenario: A member of staff is assaulted by a patient but does not want to make a formal complaint to the police._ What does the trust policy require in this situation?
- A. The matter is closed and only a Datix entry is needed
- **B. A senior staff member must still report the incident to the police, obtain a police reference number and record it in the incident report [correct]**
- C. The decision rests entirely with the member of staff and no police contact should be made
- D. The Responsible Clinician decides whether to contact the police at the next ward round
_Why:_ The policy states that if the victim does not wish to make a formal complaint, a senior staff member must still report the incident to the police, and that staff must obtain a police reference number and record it in the incident report. The Local Security Management Specialist supports staff with reporting crimes.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Prevention and Management of Violence and Aggression (Use of Force) Policy (March 2026, issue 3, review March 2029)

**Q62. [Hard]** _Scenario: A patient has just been released from a physical intervention._ What does the trust policy say about recording physical observations after a physical intervention?
- A. Observations are only needed if rapid tranquillisation was given
- B. Observations should be recorded in the nursing narrative note only
- **C. Observations must be documented on the NEWS2 physical observations chart with a NEWS calculated, and the rationale for the level of physical observations taken must also be recorded on SystmOne [correct]**
- D. Observations are optional if the patient refuses to cooperate
_Why:_ The post physical intervention section requires observations to be documented on the NEWS2 physical observations chart with a NEWS calculated, and states that the rationale for the level of physical observations taken should also be recorded on SystmOne. If there is any concern about the patient's physical health a doctor must be contacted.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Prevention and Management of Violence and Aggression (Use of Force) Policy (March 2026, issue 3, review March 2029)

**Q63. [Medium]** _Scenario: A patient is on a ward with fixed meal times, a strict daily routine and little say over day to day decisions._ How does the trust's Reducing Restrictive Practice Policy describe practices like these?
- A. Environmental restraint, which is outside the scope of the policy
- **B. Emotional restraint, including 'soft cultural context' practices that limit individuality, personalisation and choice [correct]**
- C. Relational security, which is encouraged as good practice
- D. Procedural security, which only applies in secure services
_Why:_ The policy states the Trust recognises 'emotional restraint', which includes 'soft cultural context' practices that limit individuality, personalisation and choice, and names blanket rules such as fixed meal times, a strict routine and a lack of choice over day to day decisions as examples.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Reducing Restrictive Practice Policy (December 2025, issue 3, review December 2028)

**Q64. [Easy]** Which of these uses of restrictive practice does the trust's Reducing Restrictive Practice Policy specifically rule out?
- **A. Using restrictive practice to enforce rules, to punish or coerce, or as a substitute for a lack of resources [correct]**
- B. Using restrictive practice after de-escalation has been attempted and failed
- C. Using restrictive practice that has been planned with the patient in their care plan
- D. Using restrictive practice carried out by staff trained to do so
_Why:_ The policy standards state restrictive practices must not be used in any way that is degrading or abusive, and must not be used to enforce rules, to punish or coerce, or as a substitute for a lack of resources. The other options describe how the policy expects restrictive interventions to be used when they are genuinely necessary.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Reducing Restrictive Practice Policy (December 2025, issue 3, review December 2028)

**Q65. [Medium]** What does the Reducing Restrictive Practice Policy expect of local teams and wards that use restrictive practices?
- A. That they rely solely on the trust-wide policy, since local variation is not permitted
- B. That they seek written approval from the Care Quality Commission for each practice
- **C. That they have a local policy, which may sit within their operational policy, outlining their restrictive practices and blanket rules and how they opt for the least restrictive option first [correct]**
- D. That they submit a monthly return to the Executive Director of Nursing
_Why:_ The scope section states local teams and wards that use restrictive practices are expected to have a local policy, which may be included in the local operational policy, outlining the restrictive practices and blanket rules they have, the methods used to ensure the least restrictive option comes first, and how they communicate about blanket rules.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Reducing Restrictive Practice Policy (December 2025, issue 3, review December 2028)

**Q66. [Easy]** _Scenario: A patient makes a racist remark to a colleague. They are acutely unwell and clearly not in a state to have a conversation about it right now._ What does the trust's 'It's not okay' guidance say should happen?
- A. Let it go, as nothing can be done when someone is unwell
- **B. Document it in the patient record and revisit the behaviour at a later stage when the person is more stable [correct]**
- C. Move the colleague to another ward and say nothing to the patient
- D. Discharge the patient immediately
_Why:_ The guidance aims to increase reporting in the patient record and to revisit examples of discriminatory behaviour at a later stage where it is not possible to address them at the time due to delusional or compromised presentation. It is clear that discriminatory behaviour is not okay and will not be accepted.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - 'It's not okay' guidance on the Trust's approach to discriminatory behaviour (Issued October 2020)
_⚠ no review date shown on document_

**Q67. [Medium]** _Scenario: A patient has been repeatedly abusive to staff and the team is considering writing to them about it._ Under the 'It's not okay' guidance, what must happen with a letter to an individual who has been abusive to a member of staff?
- A. It is sent by the ward manager and filed only in the ward office
- B. It is sent anonymously so the patient cannot identify the staff member
- **C. Its suitability is discussed with the Area Service Manager and/or General Manager, and a copy is kept on the patient record [correct]**
- D. It must be approved by the Care Quality Commission before it is sent
_Why:_ The guidance includes a draft letter and says colleagues should discuss the suitability of this approach with their Area Service Manager and/or General Manager, and that the letter should also be kept on the patient record. Particularly complex or exceptional issues can be taken to the Acting Deputy Director of Operations for expert guidance.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - 'It's not okay' guidance on the Trust's approach to discriminatory behaviour (Issued October 2020)
_⚠ no review date shown on document_

## Trust Policy - Rapid Tranquillisation & High Dose

**Q68. [Easy]** The trust's Rapid Tranquilisation (RT) prescribing algorithm names two first-choice intramuscular (IM) options. What are they?
- A. IM olanzapine on its own, or IM aripiprazole with IM lorazepam
- **B. IM lorazepam on its own, or IM haloperidol combined with IM promethazine [correct]**
- C. IM diazepam on its own, or IM haloperidol combined with IM lorazepam
- D. IM midazolam on its own, or IM zuclopenthixol acetate
_Why:_ The trust's Rapid Tranquilisation Prescribing Algorithm gives a choice of either IM lorazepam alone or IM haloperidol combined with IM promethazine for rapid tranquilisation in adults.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Drug Management of Violence and Aggression: Policy (August 2025, issue 10, review January 2027)

**Q69. [Easy]** _Scenario: A patient is newly admitted overnight. There is almost no history available and no record of them ever having taken an antipsychotic. Rapid Tranquilisation (RT) is now needed._ Which intramuscular (IM) option does the trust algorithm say to use in this situation?
- **A. IM lorazepam [correct]**
- B. IM haloperidol combined with IM promethazine
- C. IM olanzapine
- D. IM haloperidol on its own
_Why:_ The trust algorithm states that if there is insufficient information to guide the choice of medication, or the person has not taken antipsychotic medication before, use IM lorazepam.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Drug Management of Violence and Aggression: Policy (August 2025, issue 10, review January 2027)

**Q70. [Easy]** Where does the trust policy say all medication for Rapid Tranquilisation (RT) must be prescribed from on SystmOne, and why does it matter?
- A. The Live Master Formulary, so the medication appears on the main drug chart
- B. The ward stock list, so pharmacy can reorder the medication
- **C. The RT Formulary, so that monitoring is triggered after administration [correct]**
- D. Any formulary, as long as the dose is written as 'once only'
_Why:_ The policy states that RT must be prescribed using the Rapid Tranquilisation formulary on SystmOne to ensure monitoring is triggered after administration, and that administration links to the RT questionnaire.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Drug Management of Violence and Aggression: Policy (August 2025, issue 10, review January 2027)

**Q71. [Easy]** How does the trust policy classify the administration of intramuscular (IM) Rapid Tranquilisation (RT) for incident reporting purposes?
- **A. It is a restrictive practice and must be reported on DATIX on every occasion [correct]**
- B. It only needs a DATIX report if the patient was also physically restrained
- C. It only needs a DATIX report if the patient came to harm
- D. It is recorded on the SystmOne form only, with no DATIX report needed
_Why:_ The Administration of IM Medication for Rapid Tranquilisation algorithm states in capitals that IM RT is a RESTRICTIVE PRACTICE and must be reported on DATIX on every occasion.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Drug Management of Violence and Aggression: Policy (August 2025, issue 10, review January 2027)

**Q72. [Easy]** Which set of physical observations does the trust's Rapid Tranquilisation (RT) monitoring algorithm tell you to monitor the person for?
- A. Blood pressure, pulse, respiratory rate, blood glucose, weight and level of consciousness
- **B. Side effects, blood pressure, pulse, respiratory rate, temperature, hydration and level of consciousness [correct]**
- C. Blood pressure, pulse and level of consciousness only
- D. Side effects, blood pressure, pulse, oxygen saturation and urine output
_Why:_ The trust monitoring algorithm lists side effects, blood pressure, pulse, respiratory rate, temperature, hydration and level of consciousness.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Drug Management of Violence and Aggression: Policy (August 2025, issue 10, review January 2027)

**Q73. [Easy]** What does the trust policy say about zuclopenthixol acetate (Clopixol Acuphase) in the management of violence and aggression?
- A. It is the trust's preferred option when two doses of intramuscular (IM) lorazepam have failed
- B. It may be used as Rapid Tranquilisation (RT) if a specialist pharmacist agrees
- **C. It is not an appropriate medicine for the rapid resolution of violence and aggression [correct]**
- D. It is the first choice for RT in patients with a prolonged QT interval
_Why:_ The policy states plainly that zuclopenthixol acetate is not an appropriate medicine for use in the rapid resolution of violence and aggression, and points to a separate trust guideline for its role in treating acute psychosis or mania.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Drug Management of Violence and Aggression: Policy (August 2025, issue 10, review January 2027)

**Q74. [Easy]** In the trust's pathway for using medicines in violence and aggression, when should intramuscular (IM) Rapid Tranquilisation (RT) be used rather than oral medication?
- A. Whenever the patient is agitated, because IM works faster
- **B. Only when the patient is violent and aggressive and oral medication is refused, inappropriate or has not worked adequately [correct]**
- C. Whenever a patient is detained under the Mental Health Act
- D. Only after two doses of oral medication have been given in the same shift
_Why:_ The trust pathway states that oral medication should always be offered first line, and that IM RT is for when the patient is violent and aggressive AND oral medication is refused, inappropriate or has not produced an adequate response.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Drug Management of Violence and Aggression: Policy (August 2025, issue 10, review January 2027)

**Q75. [Easy]** How does the trust policy say the first prescription for Rapid Tranquilisation (RT) should be written?
- A. As a regular twice daily prescription for 72 hours
- B. As a 'when required' (PRN) prescription with a maximum of four doses in 24 hours
- **C. As a 'once only' single dose that is not repeated until the effect of the first dose has been assessed [correct]**
- D. As a 'when required' prescription that can be repeated after the minimum interval without further review
_Why:_ The policy requires the initial prescription to be written as a 'once only' single dose, not to be repeated until the effect of the initial dose has been reviewed, with the actions documented.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Drug Management of Violence and Aggression: Policy (August 2025, issue 10, review January 2027)

**Q76. [Easy]** _Scenario: A patient on your ward received intramuscular (IM) Rapid Tranquilisation (RT) yesterday evening and needed a further dose this morning._ What medical review does the trust policy require while RT is being used?
- **A. A senior doctor or prescribing Approved Clinician (AC) review documented within 24 hours, and a senior doctor or AC review of all medication at least once a day [correct]**
- B. A review by any doctor within 72 hours
- C. A review at the next weekly ward round only
- D. A review only if the patient has a physical health problem or a poor response
_Why:_ The trust monitoring and debrief algorithm requires a senior medical or AC review documented within 24 hours, and a senior doctor or AC to review all medication at least once a day while rapid tranquilisation is being used.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Drug Management of Violence and Aggression: Policy (August 2025, issue 10, review January 2027)

**Q77. [Easy]** The trust's Standard Operating Procedure (SOP) for Rapid Tranquilisation (RT) walks you through the SystmOne clinical monitoring form. What does it tell you to do in addition to completing that form?
- A. Print the form and file it in the paper notes
- B. Email the completed form to the ward manager
- **C. Ensure a narrative is recorded in the patient's clinical record [correct]**
- D. Nothing further, as the form replaces the nursing entry
_Why:_ The SOP states in capitals that staff must ensure a narrative is recorded in the patient's clinical record, alongside completing the RT clinical monitoring form on SystmOne.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Standard Operating Procedure: Rapid Tranquilisation (Issue 1, no issue or review date completed on the document)

**Q78. [Easy]** Under the trust's High Dose Antipsychotic Treatment (HDAT) policy, who may make the decision to start a patient on high dose antipsychotic treatment?
- A. Any prescriber, provided a specialist pharmacist agrees
- **B. Only a consultant psychiatrist or a prescribing Approved Clinician (AC) [correct]**
- C. The duty doctor, if the patient is acutely disturbed
- D. The multidisciplinary team (MDT) by consensus at the weekly review
_Why:_ The HDAT policy states that only consultant psychiatrists or prescribing ACs should initiate prescribing of antipsychotics above the recommended upper limit, although prescribing may then be continued by junior doctors or non-medical prescribers within their scope of practice subject to consultant review.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Antipsychotics: High Dose Antipsychotic Treatment (HDAT) Policy (Inpatient and Community Service Users) (November 2024, issue 9, review November 2027)

**Q79. [Medium]** _Scenario: A patient needs Rapid Tranquilisation (RT). There is a note of cardiovascular disease in the record and no recent electrocardiogram (ECG) has been done._ What does the trust algorithm direct you towards?
- A. Use intramuscular (IM) haloperidol with IM promethazine at a reduced dose
- B. Use IM haloperidol on its own and arrange an ECG afterwards
- **C. Use IM lorazepam and avoid the IM haloperidol and promethazine combination [correct]**
- D. Do not give any RT until an ECG has been completed
_Why:_ The trust algorithm says that where there is evidence of cardiovascular disease including a prolonged QT interval, or no ECG has been carried out, avoid IM haloperidol combined with IM promethazine and use IM lorazepam instead.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Drug Management of Violence and Aggression: Policy (August 2025, issue 10, review January 2027)

**Q80. [Medium]** The trust's Rapid Tranquilisation (RT) monitoring algorithm sets out 'higher risk' criteria that increase the level of monitoring needed. Which of the following is listed as one of them?
- **A. The patient has taken illicit drugs or alcohol [correct]**
- B. The patient is under 25 years old
- C. The patient is detained under the Mental Health Act
- D. The patient has refused oral medication twice
_Why:_ The trust's higher risk list is: appears to be asleep or sedated, has taken illicit drugs or alcohol, has a pre-existing physical health problem, or has experienced any harm as a result of a restrictive intervention.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Drug Management of Violence and Aggression: Policy (August 2025, issue 10, review January 2027)

**Q81. [Medium]** _Scenario: After Rapid Tranquilisation (RT), a patient will not allow you anywhere near them to take a blood pressure or pulse._ What does the trust policy tell you to do?
- A. Record the observations as 'not done' and move on
- **B. Document the reasons why observations could not be completed, and any evidence that the patient's wellbeing is being maintained [correct]**
- C. Restrain the patient again so that observations can be taken
- D. Wait until the next scheduled shift handover and try then
_Why:_ The trust monitoring algorithm states that if physical observations cannot be completed, staff must document the reasons why and any evidence that the patient's wellbeing is maintained.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Drug Management of Violence and Aggression: Policy (August 2025, issue 10, review January 2027)

**Q82. [Medium]** How often does the trust policy say the patient's individualised pharmacological strategy should be reviewed, and by whom?
- A. Daily, by the ward nurse in charge
- B. At least monthly, by the responsible clinician alone
- **C. At least once a week, by a multidisciplinary team (MDT) that includes a psychiatrist or prescribing Approved Clinician (AC) and a specialist mental health pharmacist [correct]**
- D. Only when a change of medication is being proposed
_Why:_ The Individualised Pharmacological Strategy algorithm requires an MDT including a psychiatrist (consultant or speciality doctor) or prescribing AC, plus a specialist mental health pharmacist, to review the strategy at least once a week and more frequently if events are escalating.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Drug Management of Violence and Aggression: Policy (August 2025, issue 10, review January 2027)

**Q83. [Medium]** _Scenario: The immediate risks after an episode of intramuscular (IM) Rapid Tranquilisation (RT) have been managed and the ward is settled again._ Who does the trust policy say must be involved in the immediate post-incident debrief?
- **A. A nurse and a doctor [correct]**
- B. Two registered nurses
- C. The ward manager and a member of the security team
- D. The nurse in charge alone, with a doctor informed afterwards
_Why:_ The trust monitoring and debrief algorithm requires an immediate post-incident debrief including a nurse and a doctor, to identify and address physical harm, ongoing risk and the emotional impact on service users and staff, including witnesses.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Drug Management of Violence and Aggression: Policy (August 2025, issue 10, review January 2027)

**Q84. [Medium]** _Scenario: SystmOne is down across the unit and a patient has just been given intramuscular (IM) Rapid Tranquilisation (RT)._ What does the trust guidance tell you to use for the monitoring record?
- A. The standard nursing observation chart, then copy it into SystmOne when it returns
- B. The National Early Warning Score 2 (NEWS2) chart alone, as it is the only approved paper record
- C. A free text entry in the paper notes, as no paper form exists
- **D. The hard copy 'Rapid Tranquilisation - Clinical monitoring Form', found under 'approved forms' on the Trust intranet [correct]**
_Why:_ The policy states that if SystmOne is unavailable, hard copy monitoring forms can be found in 'approved forms' on the Trust intranet under the title 'Rapid Tranquilisation - Clinical monitoring Form', and reminds staff not to forget the DATIX.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Drug Management of Violence and Aggression: Policy (August 2025, issue 10, review January 2027)

**Q85. [Medium]** _Scenario: A patient given intramuscular (IM) haloperidol develops severe painful muscular stiffness and their eyes roll upwards._ What management does the trust policy's side-effect table give for acute dystonia?
- **A. Intramuscular (IM) procyclidine 5 to 10mg [correct]**
- B. Oral diazepam 10mg
- C. IM promethazine 25mg
- D. Withhold all medication and observe for one hour
_Why:_ The trust's management of side-effects table lists acute dystonia, including oculogyric crisis, as managed with procyclidine IM 5 to 10mg.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Drug Management of Violence and Aggression: Policy (August 2025, issue 10, review January 2027)

**Q86. [Medium]** Under the trust's Rapid Tranquilisation (RT) Standard Operating Procedure (SOP), when should the debriefing form and checklist be completed with the patient?
- A. Before the end of the shift on which RT was given
- **B. Within 48 hours of ceasing the intervention, and if that is not possible then as soon as possible with the reason for delay documented [correct]**
- C. Within 7 days, at the next multidisciplinary team (MDT) review
- D. Within 72 hours, but only if the patient asks for a debrief
_Why:_ The SOP states the debriefing form and checklist should be completed with the patient within 48 hours of ceasing the intervention, and if that is not possible then as soon as possible, documenting the reason for the delay.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Standard Operating Procedure: Rapid Tranquilisation (Issue 1, no issue or review date completed on the document)

**Q87. [Medium]** In the SystmOne Rapid Tranquilisation (RT) template described in the trust Standard Operating Procedure (SOP), how are the post-injection monitoring observations recorded?
- A. Free text only, in the presentation box
- B. On a separate paper observation chart scanned in later
- **C. Using the National Early Warning Score 2 (NEWS2) template, opened from the monitoring form [correct]**
- D. By copying the figures into the medication chart notes field
