- B. Before the next Multidisciplinary Team (MDT) meeting
- **C. As soon as possible after the search and before the end of your period of duty [correct]**
- D. Within 72 hours, in line with the incident reporting timescale
_Why:_ The policy states the nurse accountable for the search must complete a Datix incident form, complete the search record for the clinical notes and make an electronic patient record entry as soon as possible after the search and before the end of their period of duty.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Searching of Service Users: Room, Possessions, Lockers, Personal Property and Ward Area Policy and Procedure (July 2025, issue 7, review July 2028)

**Q176. [Medium]** _Scenario: A patient's belongings are removed during a search._ Under the trust's searching policy, what must the patient be told and given?
- A. Nothing, as the items are no longer their property once removed
- B. Only a verbal explanation, recorded in the ward diary
- C. A written apology and a copy of the search policy
- **D. Why the items were removed, a receipt for them, where they will be stored and when they will be returned, all recorded in the electronic patient record [correct]**
_Why:_ The policy requires that where belongings are removed during a search the patient is told why, given a receipt, told where the items will be stored and when they will be returned, and that this is recorded in the electronic patient record.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Searching of Service Users: Room, Possessions, Lockers, Personal Property and Ward Area Policy and Procedure (July 2025, issue 7, review July 2028)

**Q177. [Hard]** _Scenario: A bottle of vodka is found in a patient's room during a search._ How does the trust's searching policy say alcohol found during a search should be disposed of?
- **A. Poured down the sink by two staff members, with the patient present if they wish, and the disposal documented in the clinical record [correct]**
- B. Locked in the ward safe and returned to the patient on discharge
- C. Handed to the Local Security Management Specialist for disposal
- D. Sent to the pharmacy department for destruction
_Why:_ The removal of items table in the policy states alcohol is disposed of by two staff members, with the patient present if they wish, poured down the sink, and the disposal documented in the clinical record.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Searching of Service Users: Room, Possessions, Lockers, Personal Property and Ward Area Policy and Procedure (July 2025, issue 7, review July 2028)

**Q178. [Hard]** _Scenario: A small quantity of a substance suspected to be an illegal drug is found during a room search._ What does the trust's searching policy say must happen to suspected illegal drugs found on the ward?
- A. They are flushed away immediately by two staff and a Datix completed
- B. They are bagged and kept in the nurses' office until the ward round
- **C. They are never returned to the patient, stored in a controlled drugs (CD) cabinet, the Chief Pharmacist is notified, an incident form is completed and police are contacted if the quantity is suspect [correct]**
- D. They are handed straight to the visiting relative to remove from the premises
_Why:_ The policy's removal of items table states suspected illegal drugs are never returned, are stored in a CD cabinet, the Chief Pharmacist is notified, an incident form is completed, and the police are contacted if the quantity is suspect.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Searching of Service Users: Room, Possessions, Lockers, Personal Property and Ward Area Policy and Procedure (July 2025, issue 7, review July 2028)

**Q179. [Medium]** _Scenario: A bladed item is found during a search of a communal area._ What does the trust's searching policy say is provided in each clinical area for securing bladed weapons?
- A. A sealed evidence envelope kept in the drug trolley
- **B. Bladed weapons tubes, with the Health and Safety department contacted for collection [correct]**
- C. A yellow sharps bin, emptied by the domestic team
- D. A lockable knife box held by the ward manager
_Why:_ The policy states bladed weapons tubes are available in each clinical area for the safe securing of such items, that the Health and Safety department is contacted for collection, and that additional tubes can be obtained from the Health and Safety and Risk department.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Searching of Service Users: Room, Possessions, Lockers, Personal Property and Ward Area Policy and Procedure (July 2025, issue 7, review July 2028)

**Q180. [Medium]** _Scenario: Two staff are about to search a patient's bedroom._ How does the trust's searching policy say the two staff should move through the room?
- A. They should split the room in half and each search their own side to save time
- B. One should search while the other waits outside the door
- **C. The lead staff member searches and the second follows behind, searching the exact same area, so that nothing is missed [correct]**
- D. They should search only the areas the patient points out
_Why:_ The policy states the leading staff member commences the search with the second following and searching the exact same area, so staff follow each other around the room rather than searching separate areas, and the registered nurse leads the room search.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Searching of Service Users: Room, Possessions, Lockers, Personal Property and Ward Area Policy and Procedure (July 2025, issue 7, review July 2028)

**Q181. [Hard]** _Scenario: A ward is considering introducing regular unannounced searches as a deterrent._ Under the trust's searching policy, what can random or pro-active searches cover, and who monitors how often they happen?
- **A. Only the internal and external environment owned or occupied by the trust, not personal possessions or body searches, with frequency decided locally and monitored by Service Line Managers or Divisional Nurses [correct]**
- B. Everything including patients' possessions and person, with frequency set nationally by NHS England
- C. Only bedrooms and lockers, with frequency agreed by the Responsible Clinician
- D. Only visitors' bags, with frequency monitored by the Local Security Management Specialist
_Why:_ The policy limits random and pro-active searching to the internal and external environment, that is trust owned or occupied property including corridors, toilets, bathrooms, sleeping areas, storerooms and lounges, and excludes personal possessions and body searches; frequency is decided locally and monitored by Service Line Managers or Divisional Nurses.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Searching of Service Users: Room, Possessions, Lockers, Personal Property and Ward Area Policy and Procedure (July 2025, issue 7, review July 2028)

**Q182. [Easy]** How does the trust's searching policy define a body search?
- A. A search requiring the removal of all clothing in a private room
- **B. A search of the patient's person and clothing by a minimum of two staff, with no removal of clothing except outer wear such as a coat or jacket, plus headwear and footwear [correct]**
- C. A visual inspection only, with no physical contact permitted
- D. A search carried out by the police at the request of the nurse in charge
_Why:_ The policy defines a body search as a minimum of two staff searching the person and clothing, with no removal of clothing except outer wear such as a coat or jacket, plus headwear and footwear; removing all or most clothing would be a strip search.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Searching of Service Users: Room, Possessions, Lockers, Personal Property and Ward Area Policy and Procedure (July 2025, issue 7, review July 2028)

**Q183. [Medium]** _Scenario: A visitor is suspected of bringing drugs onto the ward and refuses to allow their bag or person to be searched._ What does the trust's searching policy say staff should do?
- A. Allow the visit to go ahead but supervise it closely
- B. Call the police and detain the visitor until they arrive
- C. Search the bag anyway, since the visitor is on trust property
- **D. Deny them access to the ward and ask them to leave, explaining the reasons, submit a Datix, and inform the patient they had come to visit why access was refused [correct]**
_Why:_ The policy says a visitor who refuses to have their baggage and person searched is denied access to the ward and asked to leave with the reasons explained, a Datix is submitted, the patient is told why the visitor was refused access, and the matron or senior nurse and the patient's senior medical staff are informed.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Searching of Service Users: Room, Possessions, Lockers, Personal Property and Ward Area Policy and Procedure (July 2025, issue 7, review July 2028)

**Q184. [Hard]** _Scenario: The nursing team believes a search by force is necessary, but the consultant objects on clinical grounds._ How does the trust's searching policy say this disagreement should be resolved?
- A. The consultant's decision is final and no further escalation is possible
- **B. The matter is referred to the Medical Director, or the On Call Director, for an immediate decision, with the patient kept under observation while this is resolved [correct]**
- C. The nurse in charge may proceed once two registered nurses agree
- D. The matter is deferred to the next Multidisciplinary Team (MDT) meeting
_Why:_ Appendix 1 of the policy states that if the consultant objects on clinical grounds and staff still want to proceed, the matter is referred to the Medical Director or On Call Director for an immediate decision, and the patient is kept under observation and isolated if needed while this is resolved.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Searching of Service Users: Room, Possessions, Lockers, Personal Property and Ward Area Policy and Procedure (July 2025, issue 7, review July 2028)

**Q185. [Medium]** Where is search training delivered from within the trust, according to the searching policy?
- A. By the Local Security Management Specialist at ward induction
- B. By an external security contractor once a year
- **C. By the Positive and Safe Team, as part of the 5 day induction and refresher training [correct]**
- D. By the Health and Safety and Risk department during statutory and mandatory training
_Why:_ The policy states that search training is delivered by the Positive and Safe Team as part of the 5 day induction and refresher training, because of the heightened risks associated with inpatient facilities.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Searching of Service Users: Room, Possessions, Lockers, Personal Property and Ward Area Policy and Procedure (July 2025, issue 7, review July 2028)

**Q186. [Hard]** _Scenario: A search has just been completed on a patient who withheld their consent throughout._ What does the trust's searching policy require in addition to the usual documentation?
- A. A safeguarding referral to the local authority
- **B. A post-incident review, which should follow every search undertaken where consent has been withheld [correct]**
- C. An automatic referral to the Care Quality Commission
- D. A formal apology letter to the patient from the ward manager
_Why:_ The policy states that where a patient physically resists a personal search, physical intervention should normally only proceed on the basis of a multidisciplinary assessment unless urgently required, and that a post-incident review should follow every search undertaken where consent has been withheld.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Searching of Service Users: Room, Possessions, Lockers, Personal Property and Ward Area Policy and Procedure (July 2025, issue 7, review July 2028)

**Q187. [Easy]** Which of these is listed as a PROHIBITED item on the trust's restricted and prohibited items list for working age adult acute inpatient services?
- A. A wet razor
- B. A pair of scissors
- **C. Plastic carrier bags [correct]**
- D. A phone charger of standard size
_Why:_ Plastic carrier bags appear in the prohibited list alongside items such as wire coat hangers, glass and ceramic items and extension leads. Wet razors, scissors and standard size chargers are on the controlled list, meaning they can be accessed with risk assessment and secure storage.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Restricted and Prohibited Items List for Working Age Adult Acute Inpatient Services (no issue or review date shown on the document)

**Q188. [Medium]** _Scenario: A patient asks whether they can bring their games console and its controller onto the ward._ How does the trust's restricted and prohibited items list treat electricals such as extension leads, ethernet cables and console controllers, and USB pen drives?
- **A. Prohibited, meaning they should not be accessible throughout the admission [correct]**
- B. Controlled, so they can be issued from a locked cupboard on request
- C. Advisory only, so the patient may keep them at their own risk
- D. Permitted provided they have been tested by the estates department
_Why:_ The list places electricals such as extension leads, ethernet cables and console controllers, along with USB pen drives or other devices that can store electronic data, in the prohibited category, so they are not to be accessible throughout an admission.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Restricted and Prohibited Items List for Working Age Adult Acute Inpatient Services (no issue or review date shown on the document)

**Q189. [Hard]** _Scenario: A patient wants to keep a deodorant in their room._ What does the trust's restricted and prohibited items list say about deodorants and aerosols?
- A. All deodorants are prohibited on acute inpatient wards
- **B. They are controlled items, but a roll on can be held by the patient [correct]**
- C. They are advisory items only, so the ward takes no responsibility for them
- D. They may be held by the patient in any form provided a risk assessment is recorded
_Why:_ Deodorants and aerosols are on the controlled list, meaning they are risk assessed at the time of request and stored securely, but the list specifically notes that a roll on can be held by the patient.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Restricted and Prohibited Items List for Working Age Adult Acute Inpatient Services (no issue or review date shown on the document)

**Q190. [Hard]** _Scenario: A patient arrives on the ward with a large sum of cash in their bag._ What does the trust's restricted and prohibited items list advise about cash?
- A. All cash must be surrendered to the ward safe on admission
- B. Cash is a controlled item and must be issued in small amounts by staff
- C. Cash is prohibited on acute inpatient wards
- **D. Large amounts of cash are an advisory item, with the suggestion that anything over 20 pounds is held in the patient's bank and withdrawn as required [correct]**
_Why:_ Large amounts of cash sit in the advisory category alongside jewellery, expensive watches and sentimental items, with the list suggesting that amounts over 20 pounds are held in the patient's bank and withdrawn as required, and noting the ward cannot be held responsible for advisory items.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Restricted and Prohibited Items List for Working Age Adult Acute Inpatient Services (no issue or review date shown on the document)

**Q191. [Medium]** _Scenario: A patient is admitted carrying an item that appears on the prohibited list._ What does the trust's restricted and prohibited items list say should happen to prohibited items a patient arrives with?
- **A. Assess whether it is appropriate to lock them away securely and return them on discharge, or whether they need to be returned to family and carers to take home [correct]**
- B. Dispose of them immediately and record the disposal on Datix
- C. Hand them to the Local Security Management Specialist in all cases
- D. Store them in the patient's bedside locker with a signed disclaimer
_Why:_ The list states that if an individual arrives with prohibited items, staff should assess whether it is appropriate for them to be securely locked away and returned on discharge, or whether they need to go home with family and carers.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Restricted and Prohibited Items List for Working Age Adult Acute Inpatient Services (no issue or review date shown on the document)

**Q192. [Hard]** _Scenario: The team decides during a weekday afternoon that a patient's mobile phone needs to be removed._ Under the trust's mobile and camera phones policy, whose agreement must the documented plan of care include?
- A. Any two registered nurses on duty at the time
- B. The nurse in charge and the ward manager
- **C. A Multidisciplinary Team (MDT) plan including at least one of the patient's Responsible Clinician or nominated deputy, the matron, or the area service manager [correct]**
- D. The Local Security Management Specialist and the Head of Patient Safety
_Why:_ The policy requires any removal of a mobile phone to be part of a documented plan of care agreed by a multidisciplinary team including at least one of the Responsible Clinician or nominated deputy, the matron, or the area service manager, within working hours.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Mobile and Camera Phones in Inpatient and Community Clinic Areas Policy and Procedure (March 2024, issue 7, review March 2027)

**Q193. [Hard]** _Scenario: A phone needs to be removed from a patient at 10pm on a night shift._ What does the trust's mobile and camera phones policy allow out of hours, and how often must the decision then be reviewed?
- A. No phone can be removed outside 09.00 to 17.00 under any circumstances
- **B. A bleep holder can make the decision, it must be reviewed by the Multidisciplinary Team (MDT) as soon as practicable, and the decision must be reviewed regularly with a minimum of daily [correct]**
- C. The nurse in charge can decide, with review at the next weekly ward round
- D. Two registered nurses can decide, with review within 72 hours
_Why:_ The policy states that outside usual working hours of 09.00 to 17.00 a bleep holder can make the decision to remove a mobile phone, that it must be reviewed as part of an MDT meeting as soon as is practically possible, and that any decision to remove a phone must be regularly reviewed with a minimum of daily.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Mobile and Camera Phones in Inpatient and Community Clinic Areas Policy and Procedure (March 2024, issue 7, review March 2027)

**Q194. [Medium]** _Scenario: A patient hands in a mobile phone charger they have brought from home and asks to use it on the ward._ What does the trust's mobile and camera phones policy require before that charger can be used?
- A. Nothing, provided the patient uses it only in their own bedroom
- B. A visual check by the nurse in charge and an entry in the property sheet
- **C. The charger must be handed to staff for checking by an electrician from the Estates department before use, and is kept by staff because chargers are a ligature risk [correct]**
- D. The charger must be replaced with a ward charger and the patient's own charger destroyed
_Why:_ The policy states that where a phone cannot be charged off site, the charger must be handed to staff for checking by an electrician from the Estates department before use, that chargers constitute a ligature risk, and that they should be kept by staff and used in a secure environment to which service users do not have unsupervised access.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Mobile and Camera Phones in Inpatient and Community Clinic Areas Policy and Procedure (March 2024, issue 7, review March 2027)

**Q195. [Medium]** _Scenario: A patient is seen taking photographs of other patients in the ward lounge and refuses to delete them when asked._ What does the trust's mobile and camera phones policy say staff should do?
- **A. Trust staff will retrieve the phone and delete the images, complete an incident form, and remove the phone to safe storage until it is reviewed at the next clinical meeting [correct]**
- B. Leave the images in place and raise it at the next ward round
- C. Report the patient to the police before any other action
- D. Ask the patient's relatives to persuade them to delete the images
_Why:_ The policy says service users found to be using the video, photography or recording facility are asked to delete the images, and if they refuse staff will retrieve the phone and delete them; an incident form must be completed and the phone removed to safe storage until reviewed at the next clinical meeting.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Mobile and Camera Phones in Inpatient and Community Clinic Areas Policy and Procedure (March 2024, issue 7, review March 2027)

**Q196. [Medium]** _Scenario: A visitor is filming in the ward corridor and refuses to delete the footage when challenged._ What does the trust's mobile and camera phones policy say about visitors who refuse to delete images?
- A. Staff may confiscate the visitor's phone and delete the footage themselves
- **B. The situation must be escalated to senior management who decide whether it requires reporting to the police, and the visitor may be asked to leave the clinical area with the Multidisciplinary Team (MDT) able to refuse further visits [correct]**
- C. The visitor should be asked to sign a confidentiality agreement instead
- D. Nothing can be done, as visitors are not bound by trust policy
_Why:_ The policy says visitors must delete images in the presence of staff, and if they refuse the situation is escalated to senior management who decide whether to report it to the police; the visitor will be asked to leave the clinical area and the MDT may decide to refuse entry to further visits.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Mobile and Camera Phones in Inpatient and Community Clinic Areas Policy and Procedure (March 2024, issue 7, review March 2027)

**Q197. [Hard]** _Scenario: A patient refuses to hand over their phone after a decision has been made that it must be removed._ Under the trust's mobile and camera phones policy, who must be contacted and what must be considered?
- A. The nurse in charge alone, who decides whether to search the patient
- B. The Local Security Management Specialist, who decides whether to involve the police
- **C. The area service manager or nominated deputy and the Responsible Clinician or nominated deputy, who decide whether the person should be discharged for refusing to comply with trust policy [correct]**
- D. The on-call pharmacist, who decides whether the phone can be stored securely
_Why:_ The policy states that if the service user refuses to hand over the phone, the area service manager or nominated deputy and the Responsible Clinician or nominated deputy must be contacted and a decision taken about whether the person should be discharged for refusing to comply with trust policy, with detention under the Mental Health Act considered if discharge is not clinically appropriate.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Mobile and Camera Phones in Inpatient and Community Clinic Areas Policy and Procedure (March 2024, issue 7, review March 2027)

**Q198. [Easy]** What does the trust's mobile and camera phones policy say about staff carrying their own mobile phones in clinical areas?
- A. Staff may carry and use their phones freely as long as they are on silent
- **B. Staff must restrict personal use to emergency and urgent issues in designated areas only, and must not carry phones around clinical areas while on duty unless agreed with their line manager [correct]**
- C. Staff phones must be left in a locker at the start of every shift with no exceptions
- D. Staff may carry phones but only for taking clinical photographs
_Why:_ The policy states staff must restrict their personal use of mobile phones to emergency and urgent issues, only in designated areas, and must not carry their phones around within clinical areas while on duty unless this has been agreed with their line manager.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Mobile and Camera Phones in Inpatient and Community Clinic Areas Policy and Procedure (March 2024, issue 7, review March 2027)

**Q199. [Medium]** _Scenario: A parcel arrives on the ward addressed to a patient._ What does the trust's Standard Operating Procedure (SOP) on managing deliveries say staff should do first?
- A. Open the parcel before telling the patient it has arrived
- B. Return the parcel to the sender unless it was agreed in advance
- **C. Record the receipt of the parcel and promptly inform the patient that it has arrived [correct]**
- D. Hold the parcel until the next ward round for a Multidisciplinary Team (MDT) decision
_Why:_ The deliveries SOP states that on initial receipt staff should record the receipt of any parcel or delivery for a patient and promptly inform the patient of its arrival, before asking whether they consent to open it in the presence of staff.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Managing Deliveries to Inpatient Mental Health Wards at Derbyshire Healthcare NHS Foundation Trust SOP (April 2025, issue 1, review April 2028)

**Q200. [Hard]** _Scenario: A patient declines to open their parcel in front of staff, and there is no specific concern about its contents._ What does the trust's deliveries Standard Operating Procedure (SOP) say happens next?
- **A. The parcel is handed to the patient, unless there is a justified and lawful reason to intervene [correct]**
- B. The parcel is opened by two staff members without the patient present
- C. The parcel is held in the ward office until the patient agrees to open it with staff
- D. The parcel is returned to the sender and the patient informed
_Why:_ The SOP states that if a patient refuses to open the parcel in the presence of staff, the parcel should be handed to them unless there is a justified and lawful reason to intervene, reflecting the Article 8 right to correspondence and private life.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Managing Deliveries to Inpatient Mental Health Wards at Derbyshire Healthcare NHS Foundation Trust SOP (April 2025, issue 1, review April 2028)

**Q201. [Medium]** _Scenario: A patient asks whether they are allowed to order things online to be delivered to the ward._ What is the trust's stated position on deliveries in its Standard Operating Procedure (SOP)?
- A. Deliveries are banned on all inpatient mental health wards
- B. Deliveries are allowed only for detained patients
- C. Deliveries must be approved in advance by the Responsible Clinician
- **D. The trust prefers patients refrain from having items delivered, but patients are not prohibited from receiving deliveries, and some items are expressly prohibited [correct]**
_Why:_ The SOP gives staff wording to use with patients, stating the trust prefers they refrain from having items delivered for reasons of safety, security and a therapeutic environment, but that they are not prohibited from receiving deliveries, and that some items are expressly prohibited.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Managing Deliveries to Inpatient Mental Health Wards at Derbyshire Healthcare NHS Foundation Trust SOP (April 2025, issue 1, review April 2028)

**Q202. [Medium]** _Scenario: A patient asks for a disposable razor at the start of an early shift._ What does the trust's Standard Operating Procedure (SOP) on the use of sharps and razors require before a razor is allocated?
- A. A check of the patient's historical risk factors alone
- B. Written consent from the patient's Responsible Clinician
- **C. A Mental State Examination assessing current mental state and risk factors, which determines whether the razor is supervised, unsupervised or not provided [correct]**
- D. A Datix incident form recording the request
_Why:_ The SOP requires a Mental State Examination of current mental state and risk factors before allocating a razor, noting that known historical risk factors can aid the assessment but should not by themselves be the final determining factor, and that this assessment decides whether use is supervised, unsupervised or not appropriate.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Use of Sharps (Razors) on Acute Inpatient Wards and the Beeches Unit Standard Operating Procedure (version 2, ratified by Working Age Adult COAT; no issue or review date shown)

**Q203. [Hard]** _Scenario: A patient has needed supervised razor use for several weeks._ How often does the trust's sharps and razors Standard Operating Procedure (SOP) say the care plan for ongoing supervised razor use must be reviewed?
- **A. Weekly, or sooner as required [correct]**
- B. Daily, at every handover
- C. Monthly, at the Multidisciplinary Team (MDT) meeting
- D. Only when the patient's observation level changes
_Why:_ The SOP states that where supervision is required for a considerable amount of time this must be clearly documented in the care plan and reviewed weekly or sooner as required, with risk assessments updated to reflect any changing risks.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Use of Sharps (Razors) on Acute Inpatient Wards and the Beeches Unit Standard Operating Procedure (version 2, ratified by Working Age Adult COAT; no issue or review date shown)

**Q204. [Medium]** Under the trust's sharps and razors Standard Operating Procedure (SOP), what must staff use when handing out razors, and who oversees returns?
- A. A note in the patient's daily record, with the nurse in charge checking at the end of the week
- **B. The allocation sheet in Appendix 1 of the SOP, with a staff member assigned each shift to monitor allocation and check all razors have been handed in and signed back in [correct]**
- C. The ward diary, with the ward manager auditing monthly
- D. The property book only, with no separate signing in and out record
_Why:_ The SOP states that staff handing out sharps or razors must use the allocation sheet in Appendix 1 and that all razors allocated must be logged, and that a member of staff is assigned each shift, via the shift planner, to monitor allocation and check all razors are handed in and signed back in.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Use of Sharps (Razors) on Acute Inpatient Wards and the Beeches Unit Standard Operating Procedure (version 2, ratified by Working Age Adult COAT; no issue or review date shown)

**Q205. [Easy]** _Scenario: A patient does not return a razor at the end of the agreed time period._ What does the trust's sharps and razors Standard Operating Procedure (SOP) say must happen?
- A. Wait until the end of the shift and record it on the allocation sheet
- B. Complete a Datix form and hand over to the next shift
- **C. The Nurse in Charge (NiC) must be informed immediately, and the NiC will determine if a search is required [correct]**
- D. Replace the razor and note the loss in the patient's property book
_Why:_ The SOP states that if a patient fails to return the razor, the razor is tampered with, or the razor is missing, the Nurse in Charge must be informed immediately and will determine whether a search is required under the trust's searching policy.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Use of Sharps (Razors) on Acute Inpatient Wards and the Beeches Unit Standard Operating Procedure (version 2, ratified by Working Age Adult COAT; no issue or review date shown)

**Q206. [Hard]** _Scenario: A patient buys their own electric razor to use during their admission._ What does the trust's sharps and razors Standard Operating Procedure (SOP) require for personal electric razors?
- **A. They must meet electrical safety guidelines including testing and tagging by the trust, be returned to staff for storage labelled with the patient's name, must not be shared with other patients, and must be charged in the designated areas provided on the ward [correct]**
- B. They may be kept and charged freely in the patient's bedroom with no restrictions
- C. They are prohibited on acute inpatient wards and must be sent home
- D. They may be shared between patients provided the head is cleaned between uses
_Why:_ The SOP requires personal electric razors to meet electrical safety guidelines including testing and tagging by the trust, to be returned to staff for storage clearly labelled with the patient's name, never to be shared with other patients because of cross infection risk, and to be charged in the designated areas provided on the ward.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Use of Sharps (Razors) on Acute Inpatient Wards and the Beeches Unit Standard Operating Procedure (version 2, ratified by Working Age Adult COAT; no issue or review date shown)

**Q207. [Easy]** According to the trust's Standard Operating Procedure (SOP) on searches of a person, where must every search of a person be recorded?
- A. In the ward handover book only
- **B. On SystmOne, the trust's electronic patient record [correct]**
- C. On a paper search form kept in the ward office
- D. On Datix only, with no clinical record entry needed
_Why:_ The SOP states that any search of a person will need to be recorded onto SystmOne, and that the process for searching a person is the same for all inpatient units in the trust so that regular, cover and bank staff all follow the same steps.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Searches of a Person SOP (issue 1, no issue or review date shown)

**Q208. [Medium]** _Scenario: You are taking in a patient's watch and wallet for safe keeping on admission._ Under the trust's personal possessions Standard Operating Procedure (SOP), where on SystmOne is this recorded, and what must be entered?
- A. Under Clinical Notes, recording the item only
- B. Under Risk Assessment, recording the item and its estimated value
- **C. Under Patient Possessions in the Administrative tree, recording the item and the location it will be placed in [correct]**
- D. Under Care Plans, recording the item and the date it will be returned
_Why:_ The SOP directs staff to retrieve the patient from ward view, select Patient Possessions from the Administrative tree, record the possession, type in the item and the location it will be placed in, and save; returning an item is done by right clicking it and selecting return possession, which records the date and time returned.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Personal Possessions and Processes SOP (issue 1, no issue or review date shown)

## Trust Policy - Mental Health Act (local process)

**Q209. [Easy]** _Scenario: An Approved Mental Health Professional (AMHP) arrives on the ward at 9pm with section papers for a new admission._ Under the trust's Receipt and Scrutiny policy, which staff are authorised to receive and accept section papers on behalf of the Hospital Managers?
- A. Any registered nurse or health care assistant on duty
- **B. Qualified nursing staff at Band 5 and above [correct]**
- C. Only the ward manager or matron
- D. Only a Mental Health Act (MHA) Administrator
_Why:_ The trust delegates receipt of section papers to qualified nursing staff Band 5 and above, all of whom must have had specific training on receiving and accepting MHA paperwork.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Mental Health Act 1983 Receipt, Scrutiny and Management of Section Papers Policy and Procedure (December 2024, issue 04, review December 2027 (minor amendment June 2025))

**Q210. [Medium]** Once a Band 5 nurse has established that section papers for a Section 3 admission are legal, which form do they complete to formally accept the papers on behalf of the Hospital Managers?
- **A. Form H3 [correct]**
- B. Form H1
- C. Form H4
- D. Form A11
_Why:_ The trust policy directs that Form H3 is completed for sections 2, 3 and 4 once the legality of the papers has been established.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Mental Health Act 1983 Receipt, Scrutiny and Management of Section Papers Policy and Procedure (December 2024, issue 04, review December 2027 (minor amendment June 2025))

**Q211. [Hard]** _Scenario: An informal patient on the ward has been placed under a doctor's holding power and the paperwork has just been handed to the nurse in charge._ Which form does the trust policy say the receiving nurse completes to accept papers for a Section 5(2)?
- A. Form H3 in full
- B. Part 1 of Form H4
- **C. Part 2 of Form H1 [correct]**
- D. Form A11
_Why:_ The policy lists Part 2 of Form H1 as the acceptance record for Section 5(2), separate from Form H3 which covers sections 2, 3 and 4.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Mental Health Act 1983 Receipt, Scrutiny and Management of Section Papers Policy and Procedure (December 2024, issue 04, review December 2027 (minor amendment June 2025))

**Q212. [Easy]** Mental Health Act (MHA) Administrators in this trust are based on how many sites, and where?
- A. One central site at Kingsway House, Kingsway Hospital, Derby
- B. One administrator is based on each inpatient ward
- C. Three sites, one for each division of the trust
- **D. Two sites: the Derwent Unit, Royal Hospital, Chesterfield and Kingsway House, Kingsway Hospital, Derby [correct]**
_Why:_ The policy names two MHA administration bases: the Derwent Unit at the Royal Hospital in Chesterfield, and Kingsway House at Kingsway Hospital in Derby.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Mental Health Act 1983 Receipt, Scrutiny and Management of Section Papers Policy and Procedure (December 2024, issue 04, review December 2027 (minor amendment June 2025))

**Q213. [Hard]** _Scenario: You are checking the papers for a Section 2 admission and notice the two medical examinations were seven days apart._ According to the trust's list of errors, how should this be treated?
- A. It is a rectifiable error and can be corrected within 14 days
- B. It is acceptable because the examinations were within 14 days of the application
- **C. It cannot be rectified and would render the detention invalid [correct]**
- D. It only matters for Section 3, not Section 2
_Why:_ The trust policy lists medical examinations more than 5 clear days apart (sections 2 and 3) among the errors which cannot be rectified and which render the detention invalid.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Mental Health Act 1983 Receipt, Scrutiny and Management of Section Papers Policy and Procedure (December 2024, issue 04, review December 2027 (minor amendment June 2025))

**Q214. [Medium]** _Scenario: The patient's surname is spelled two different ways across the application and the medical recommendations._ How does the trust policy classify this error?
- **A. Rectifiable by the author within 14 days of the patient being admitted on a section [correct]**
- B. Fundamental, so a fresh application must be made immediately
- C. Rectifiable, but only by the Mental Health Act (MHA) office, not the author
- D. Not an error at all, as Section 6(3) allows staff to act without further proof
_Why:_ The policy lists a patient's name and address not being fully completed, inaccurate or not identical on all forms among the errors rectifiable under Section 15 within 14 days of admission.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Mental Health Act 1983 Receipt, Scrutiny and Management of Section Papers Policy and Procedure (December 2024, issue 04, review December 2027 (minor amendment June 2025))

**Q215. [Medium]** _Scenario: A nurse establishes that a newly admitted patient's detention papers are invalid._ Which set of actions does the trust policy require of nursing staff?
- A. Wait until the Mental Health Act (MHA) office opens the next working day before telling anyone
- **B. Notify the Responsible Clinician or Approved Clinician, the Approved Mental Health Professional (AMHP) and the MHA office, tell the patient they are no longer detained, and consider whether Section 5(4) or 5(2) is needed [correct]**
- C. Notify only the Responsible Clinician and continue to treat the patient as detained until the papers are corrected
- D. Ask the applicant to complete fresh papers and say nothing to the patient until they arrive
_Why:_ The policy sets out that nursing staff notify the Responsible Clinician or Approved Clinician, the AMHP and the MHA office, inform the patient they are no longer detained and of their right to legal advice, and give consideration to the use of Section 5(4) or 5(2).
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Mental Health Act 1983 Receipt, Scrutiny and Management of Section Papers Policy and Procedure (December 2024, issue 04, review December 2027 (minor amendment June 2025))

**Q216. [Hard]** Under the trust's protocol for retention and storage of section papers, how long are hard copies kept once scanned, and what must happen before they are destroyed?
- **A. A maximum of 28 days, and a second check must be completed by a different member of the team before the papers are cross shredded [correct]**
- B. A maximum of 7 days, after which the ward manager signs them off for shredding
- C. Six months, held in the ward's locked Mental Health Act (MHA) file
- D. They are kept indefinitely, as the paper copy remains the legal original
_Why:_ The trust protocol retains hard copies for a maximum of 28 days so the scan can be checked for completeness and legibility, with a second check by a different member of the team before cross shredding as confidential waste.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Mental Health Act 1983 Receipt, Scrutiny and Management of Section Papers Policy and Procedure (December 2024, issue 04, review December 2027 (minor amendment June 2025))

**Q217. [Easy]** Once section papers have been scanned onto SystmOne, which version does the trust treat as the original?
- A. The paper copy filed in the Mental Health Act (MHA) office
- B. The paper copy held in the patient's ward folder
- **C. The electronic version on SystmOne [correct]**
- D. Whichever copy the Responsible Clinician signed most recently
_Why:_ The trust protocol states that once section papers are uploaded to SystmOne the electronic version becomes the original, and hard copies are then securely destroyed.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Mental Health Act 1983 Receipt, Scrutiny and Management of Section Papers Policy and Procedure (December 2024, issue 04, review December 2027 (minor amendment June 2025))

**Q218. [Hard]** After the Mental Health Act (MHA) office has completed its administrative scrutiny, what happens next to the medical recommendations, and by when?
- A. They are filed on the ward and reviewed at the next care planning meeting
- **B. They are e-mailed to Section 12 approved doctors in the trust for medical scrutiny, which must happen as soon as possible and not exceed 14 days from the start of detention [correct]**
- C. They are sent to the Responsible Clinician for countersignature within 72 hours
- D. They are reviewed by the Mental Health Act Committee at its next quarterly meeting
_Why:_ The policy sets out a two-stage scrutiny: administrative scrutiny by the MHA office, then medical scrutiny by Section 12 approved doctors, which must take place as soon as possible and not exceeding 14 days since the start of the detention.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Mental Health Act 1983 Receipt, Scrutiny and Management of Section Papers Policy and Procedure (December 2024, issue 04, review December 2027 (minor amendment June 2025))

**Q219. [Easy]** _Scenario: A patient is detained on the ward at 2pm on Monday._ Under the trust's Section 132 policy, by when must the patient be informed verbally of their rights, and by whom?
- **A. Within 24 hours of detention, by a member of the nursing team [correct]**
- B. Within 72 hours of detention, by the Responsible Clinician
- C. Within 7 days, by the Mental Health Act (MHA) office
- D. At the first ward round, by the Approved Mental Health Professional (AMHP)
_Why:_ The current version of the trust policy reduced the timescale to within 24 hours of detention in hospital, given verbally by a member of the nursing team and recorded in the Electronic Patient Record (EPR).
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Mental Health Act 1983 Section 132: Patients' Rights Policy and Procedure (March 2025, issue 6, review 31 March 2028)

**Q220. [Medium]** How often does the trust's Section 132 policy say it is good practice to remind a patient of their rights?
- A. Monthly for all patients, whether detained or on a Community Treatment Order (CTO)
- **B. At least every 3 months for inpatients and every 6 months for patients on a Community Treatment Order (CTO) [correct]**
- C. At least every 6 months for inpatients and every 12 months for patients on a Community Treatment Order (CTO)
- D. Only when the patient's legal status changes
_Why:_ The policy sets a reminder of rights at least every 3 months for inpatients and every 6 months for CTO patients, with a new questionnaire completed on SystmOne each time.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Mental Health Act 1983 Section 132: Patients' Rights Policy and Procedure (March 2025, issue 6, review 31 March 2028)

**Q221. [Medium]** _Scenario: A rights questionnaire has not reached the Mental Health Act (MHA) office 30 hours after a patient was detained._ What does the trust's monitoring process say happens?
- A. Nothing until the weekly audit report is produced
- B. The Responsible Clinician is asked to complete the rights explanation instead
- **C. The Nurse in Charge of the ward is notified for follow-up, and a list of outstanding rights forms goes to the matron and senior nurses weekly [correct]**
- D. The section is treated as invalid until the questionnaire is received
_Why:_ The policy states that if a Section 132 notification is not received within 24 hours of detention the Nurse in Charge is notified for follow-up, with a weekly list of outstanding rights forms provided to the matron and senior nurses at the trust's inpatient units.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Mental Health Act 1983 Section 132: Patients' Rights Policy and Procedure (March 2025, issue 6, review 31 March 2028)

**Q222. [Easy]** _Scenario: A newly detained patient is too distressed to take in anything you say about their rights._ What do the trust policies require you to do?
- A. Leave the rights leaflet on their bed and treat the duty as discharged
- B. Ask the Mental Health Act (MHA) office to send the information to the nearest relative instead
- C. Wait until the Responsible Clinician can do it at the next ward round
- **D. Record on the Section 132 form and in the Electronic Patient Record (EPR) that they did not understand, and repeat the explanation until they fully understand [correct]**
_Why:_ Both the Section 132 policy and the Receipt and Scrutiny policy require the lack of understanding to be recorded on the Section 132 form and in the EPR, with the explanation repeated by nursing staff until the patient fully understands.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Mental Health Act 1983 Section 132: Patients' Rights Policy and Procedure (March 2025, issue 6, review 31 March 2028)

**Q223. [Medium]** Who does the trust make responsible for reviewing and checking that the rights explanation is repeated until the patient understands?
- A. The Mental Health Act (MHA) Administrator who processed the papers
- **B. The ward manager or their nominated deputy [correct]**
- C. The Responsible Clinician
- D. The Independent Mental Health Advocate (IMHA)
_Why:_ The Receipt and Scrutiny policy places responsibility on the ward manager or nominated deputy to review and check that this process is repeated until the patient understands.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Mental Health Act 1983 Receipt, Scrutiny and Management of Section Papers Policy and Procedure (December 2024, issue 04, review December 2027 (minor amendment June 2025))

**Q224. [Easy]** _Scenario: A detained patient speaks little English and you need to explain their rights._ Which interpreting arrangement does the trust's Section 132 policy point staff to?
- A. Ask a family member to interpret
- B. The Ministry of Justice interpreter service
- C. The Mental Health Act (MHA) office keeps a rota of bilingual staff
- **D. Language Line Solutions, with details on the Focus intranet home page [correct]**
_Why:_ The policy states the trust uses Language Line Solutions and that details are found on the Focus home page. The Ministry of Justice is only responsible for providing interpreters to attend a Mental Health Tribunal.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Mental Health Act 1983 Section 132: Patients' Rights Policy and Procedure (March 2025, issue 6, review 31 March 2028)

**Q225. [Easy]** _Scenario: A patient tells you during the rights discussion that they are happy for their nearest relative to be told about the detention._ Who then writes to the nearest relative, and what triggers it?
- **A. The Mental Health Act (MHA) office writes once it has been notified that the rights questionnaire has been completed [correct]**
- B. The nurse who explained the rights writes the same day
- C. The Responsible Clinician writes after the first ward round
- D. No letter is sent unless the nearest relative asks for one
_Why:_ The policy states that when the MHA office has received notification that the rights questionnaire has been completed, and the patient does not object, it sends the nearest relative a letter enclosing the relevant patient information leaflet.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Mental Health Act 1983 Section 132: Patients' Rights Policy and Procedure (March 2025, issue 6, review 31 March 2028)

**Q226. [Hard]** _Scenario: A patient in the community has been placed on a Community Treatment Order (CTO) and no Section 132A rights explanation has been recorded._ Under the trust's monitoring arrangements, who is notified if this is not recorded in the Electronic Patient Record (EPR) within the first month of the CTO commencing?
- A. The Care Quality Commission (CQC)
- **B. The Service Line Manager [correct]**
- C. The Mental Health Act Committee
- D. The patient's nearest relative
_Why:_ The Section 132 policy states that if a Section 132A explanation of rights is not recorded in the EPR within the first month of the CTO commencing, the Service Line Manager is notified for follow-up.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Mental Health Act 1983 Section 132: Patients' Rights Policy and Procedure (March 2025, issue 6, review 31 March 2028)

**Q227. [Easy]** _Scenario: A detained patient asks to go to the shop with a visitor._ Under the trust's Section 17 policy, who is responsible for making sure a Section 17 leave form has been completed before the patient leaves hospital?
- A. The Responsible Clinician, who must be on site
- B. The Mental Health Act (MHA) office
- C. The patient's named nurse, but only for escorted leave
- **D. The Nurse in Charge [correct]**
_Why:_ The policy makes the Nurse in Charge responsible for ensuring a Section 17 form has been completed before the patient is allowed to leave hospital, and for keeping the form accessible to staff at all times.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Section 17 Leave, Mental Health Act 1983 Policy and Procedure (March 2025, issue 10, review March 2028)

**Q228. [Medium]** _Scenario: It is 11pm at the weekend and a detained patient needs leave authorising. The patient's usual Responsible Clinician is not available._ What does the trust's Section 17 policy say about who can grant the leave?
- A. The Nurse in Charge may authorise it and inform the Responsible Clinician later
- B. Any doctor on site may authorise it
- **C. Only the patient's Responsible Clinician may grant Section 17 leave, and out of hours the covering doctor will be the Consultant on call [correct]**
- D. The on-call manager may authorise it in the Responsible Clinician's absence
_Why:_ The policy is explicit that only the patient's Responsible Clinician may grant Section 17 leave and that the role may not be transferred or delegated, and that out of hours the covering doctor will be the Consultant on call.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Section 17 Leave, Mental Health Act 1983 Policy and Procedure (March 2025, issue 10, review March 2028)

**Q229. [Hard]** _Scenario: A Responsible Clinician wants to grant a detained patient ten consecutive days of home leave._ What does the trust's Section 17 policy require?
- A. Leave over seven days is not permitted under any circumstances
- **B. Section 17 leave should normally be no more than seven consecutive days, and before granting more the Responsible Clinician must consider whether the patient should be discharged onto a Community Treatment Order (CTO), with that discussion recorded in the Electronic Patient Record (EPR) [correct]**
- C. Leave over seven days needs written approval from the Mental Health Act (MHA) office
- D. Leave over seven days needs approval from a Hospital Managers panel
_Why:_ The policy says Section 17 leave should normally be of short duration and not normally more than seven consecutive days, and that before exceeding this the Responsible Clinician is required to consider a CTO and record the discussion in the EPR.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Section 17 Leave, Mental Health Act 1983 Policy and Procedure (March 2025, issue 10, review March 2028)

**Q230. [Easy]** _Scenario: A patient has authorised unescorted leave but you think they are more unwell today than they were yesterday._ What does the trust's Section 17 policy say about nursing staff withholding leave?
- A. Nursing staff can never withhold leave once the Responsible Clinician has authorised it
- **B. Nursing staff may withhold leave if they consider the patient not well enough, but it is bad practice to withhold it for any reason other than a deterioration in the patient's mental state [correct]**
- C. Nursing staff may withhold leave for any operational reason, including staffing levels
- D. Leave can only be withheld with the agreement of both the Nurse in Charge and the on-call manager
_Why:_ The policy requires nursing staff to assess the patient's clinical state before each instance of leave and permits them to withhold it if the patient is not well enough, while stating it is bad practice to withhold leave for any other reason.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Section 17 Leave, Mental Health Act 1983 Policy and Procedure (March 2025, issue 10, review March 2028)

**Q231. [Medium]** _Scenario: A detained patient collapses and needs urgent transfer to the acute hospital. The Responsible Clinician cannot be reached immediately._ What does the trust's Section 17 policy say you should do?
- A. Hold the patient on the ward until written Section 17 leave is in place
- B. Send the patient and treat the transfer as a Section 19 transfer instead
- **C. Do not delay the transfer. Contact the Responsible Clinician for authorisation as soon as practicable, document any telephone authorisation in the clinical notes, and get a copy of the completed form to the acute trust as soon as practicable [correct]**
- D. Discharge the patient from their section so they can be treated informally at the acute hospital
_Why:_ The policy states that in an emergency the transfer must not be delayed, that the Responsible Clinician can authorise Section 17 leave over the telephone, that nursing staff must document the conversation in the clinical notes, and that the form must be completed at the earliest opportunity with a copy provided to the acute trust.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Section 17 Leave, Mental Health Act 1983 Policy and Procedure (March 2025, issue 10, review March 2028)

**Q232. [Hard]** _Scenario: The team is planning Section 17 leave for a detained patient to a residential care placement._ What does the trust's Section 17 policy say about funding?
- **A. Funding must be agreed by the Commissioning Manager for Mental Health and Learning Disability, it is the ward staff's responsibility to arrange this, and it cannot be agreed retrospectively [correct]**
- B. Funding is arranged by the local authority social worker after the leave has started
- C. No funding agreement is needed while the patient remains detained
- D. Funding is agreed by the Responsible Clinician as part of authorising the leave
_Why:_ The policy states that where Section 17 leave is to residential or nursing care, funding must be agreed by the Commissioning Manager for Mental Health and Learning Disability, that it is the responsibility of ward staff to arrange this, and that funding cannot be agreed retrospectively.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Section 17 Leave, Mental Health Act 1983 Policy and Procedure (March 2025, issue 10, review March 2028)

**Q233. [Hard]** _Scenario: A detained patient is being considered for their first period of extended home leave._ What is the trust's default position on assessing the home environment?
- A. A telephone call to the family is sufficient in the first instance
- B. Repeated home visits are always required before any home leave is granted
- C. The home environment is assessed only if the patient or family requests it
- **D. A face-to-face assessment in the patient's home environment is the default unless a documented risk assessment suggests otherwise, and a face-to-face assessment is needed on the first day at home if the patient is placed on extended leave [correct]**
_Why:_ The policy sets the default as a face-to-face assessment in the home environment unless a documented risk assessment suggests otherwise, and requires a face-to-face assessment on the first day at home for patients placed on extended leave.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Section 17 Leave, Mental Health Act 1983 Policy and Procedure (March 2025, issue 10, review March 2028)

**Q234. [Medium]** _Scenario: A detained patient does not come back from home leave and is refusing to return, and you know which address they are staying at._ What does the trust's Section 17 policy say about retaking them?
- A. The police will always force entry on the ward's say-so with no further authority needed
- **B. It is the responsibility of the hospital to obtain a Section 135(2) warrant, applied for at the Magistrates Court by a member of trust staff, authorising a police officer to remove the patient using proportionate force [correct]**
- C. Only an Approved Mental Health Professional (AMHP) can retake the patient, and only from a public place
- D. Nothing can be done until the patient's current period of detention expires
_Why:_ The policy states that where the patient is known to be staying at other premises and refusing to return, it is the hospital's responsibility to obtain a Section 135(2) warrant, with the application made to the Magistrates Court by a member of trust staff.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Section 17 Leave, Mental Health Act 1983 Policy and Procedure (March 2025, issue 10, review March 2028)

**Q235. [Medium]** _Scenario: You are recording a new period of Section 17 leave on SystmOne using the trust's Standard Operating Procedure (SOP)._ After entering the expected departure and return dates and any notes, which two fields does the SOP tell you to complete before selecting the Section 17 template?
- **A. 'CTO considered' and 'Approved by' [correct]**
- B. 'Risk assessed' and 'Escorted by'
- C. 'Nearest relative informed' and 'Section 132 given'
- D. 'Ward manager notified' and 'Care plan updated'
_Why:_ The trust's Section 17 SOP takes staff through Ward view, right clicking the patient, Administrative, Add Patient leave, then the expected departure and return dates, notes, the 'CTO considered' section and the 'Approved by' section, before selecting the Section 17 template.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Standard Operating Procedure: Section 17 (SystmOne) (Issue 01, no issue or review date shown on the document)

**Q236. [Hard]** _Scenario: A patient admitted under Section 4 is examined by a second doctor within the 72 hour period and the section is converted to a Section 2._ What does the trust's Section 4 policy require of the ward's paperwork?
- A. A fresh Form H3 is completed in full for the Section 2
- **B. Part 1 of Form H3 is completed on admission on Section 4, and Part 2 of Form H3 is completed when it converts to Section 2 within 72 hours [correct]**
- C. Form H1 is completed for the Section 4 and Form H3 for the Section 2
- D. No further acceptance form is needed once the original Form H3 has been signed
_Why:_ The Section 4 policy states the Nurse in Charge completes Part 1 of Form H3 on admission under Section 4, and Part 2 of Form H3 if the section is converted to a Section 2 within the 72 hour period, with the papers then sent to the Mental Health Act Administrator without delay.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Mental Health Act 1983 Section 4 Emergency Application for Detention Policy and Procedure (March 2025, issue 03, review 31 March 2028)

**Q237. [Easy]** _Scenario: A patient detained under Section 4 asks to go off the unit for an hour with a relative._ What does the trust's Section 4 policy say about Section 17 leave for this patient?
- A. It can be granted for up to 2 hours by the Nurse in Charge
- B. It can be granted by the Consultant on call only
- **C. Section 17 leave of absence is not applicable to a patient detained under Section 4 [correct]**
- D. It can be granted but only once the second medical recommendation has been received
_Why:_ Both the Section 4 policy and the Section 17 policy state that Section 17 leave does not apply to patients detained under Section 4.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Mental Health Act 1983 Section 4 Emergency Application for Detention Policy and Procedure (March 2025, issue 03, review 31 March 2028)

**Q238. [Hard]** How often does the trust monitor the use of Section 4, and through which group?
- A. Monthly, through the ward's clinical governance meeting
- B. Annually, by the Care Quality Commission (CQC)
- C. Weekly, by the Mental Health Act (MHA) office
- **D. Quarterly, by the Mental Health Act Committee [correct]**
_Why:_ The Section 4 policy states that hospital managers and local authorities should monitor the use of Section 4 to ensure it is not misused, and that this is monitored quarterly by the Mental Health Act Committee.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Mental Health Act 1983 Section 4 Emergency Application for Detention Policy and Procedure (March 2025, issue 03, review 31 March 2028)

**Q239. [Easy]** _Scenario: A ward clerk offers to scan a set of section papers straight onto the patient's SystmOne record to save time._ What does the trust's Mental Health Act (MHA) Documents Standard Operating Procedure (SOP) say?
- A. Any registered nurse may upload section papers to SystmOne
- B. The ward clerk may upload them if the Nurse in Charge countersigns
- **C. Only staff within the MHA office can record MHA information and documentation onto SystmOne [correct]**
- D. The Responsible Clinician uploads them as part of the admission review
_Why:_ The SOP is explicit that only staff within the MHA office can record MHA information and documentation onto SystmOne, and that all original section papers must be sent on receipt to the appropriate MHA office for scrutiny, processing and uploading.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Standard Operating Procedure: MHA Documents (SystmOne) (Issue 01, no issue or review date shown on the document)

**Q240. [Medium]** _Scenario: You need to check a patient's current legal status and the key dates connected to their section before handover._ Where does the trust's Mental Health Act (MHA) Documents Standard Operating Procedure (SOP) tell you to look on SystmOne?
- A. The Alerts panel on the patient's home screen
- B. The Ward view summary bar, which lists every patient's section
- C. The scanned documents folder in the patient's communications record
- **D. The MHA node in the clinical tree within the patient record, using the Show Previous Sections tick box for historical sections [correct]**
_Why:_ The SOP directs staff to the MHA node in the clinical tree to see current sections, with a view dropdown to filter sections recorded here or elsewhere, and a Show Previous Sections tick box for historical sections.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Standard Operating Procedure: MHA Documents (SystmOne) (Issue 01, no issue or review date shown on the document)

## Trust Policy - CTO, S117 & Tribunals

**Q241. [Easy]** _Scenario: A patient subject to a Community Treatment Order has been recalled and has just walked onto your ward._ Under the trust's Community Treatment Order (CTO) policy, which form must the nurse in charge complete when a recalled patient arrives on the ward?
- A. Form CTO3
- **B. Form CTO4 [correct]**
- C. Form CTO5
- D. Form CTO12
_Why:_ The policy states that when the patient arrives on the ward the nurse in charge must complete Form CTO4, which is then sent with a copy of the CTO3 to the Mental Health Act Office. CTO3 is the recall notice completed by the Responsible Clinician and CTO5 is the revocation form.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Community Treatment Order Policy MHA 1983 (June 2024, issue 06, review June 2027)

**Q242. [Medium]** A patient recalled under a Community Treatment Order (CTO) can be held for up to 72 hours. Under trust policy, what starts that 72-hour clock?
- A. The time the Responsible Clinician signed the CTO3 recall notice
- B. The time the recall notice was served on the patient
- **C. The time recorded by ward staff on Form CTO4 [correct]**
- D. Midnight following the patient's arrival
_Why:_ The policy is explicit that the 72-hour period of recall begins from the time recorded on the CTO4, which is the time the patient arrived at hospital. This same time and date is then asked for on the CTO5 if the CTO is revoked.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Community Treatment Order Policy MHA 1983 (June 2024, issue 06, review June 2027)

**Q243. [Hard]** _Scenario: A Community Treatment Order recall notice cannot be handed to the patient, so a member of the care team delivers it by hand to their usual address at noon on a Saturday._ Under the trust's Community Treatment Order (CTO) policy, when is that recall notice deemed to have been served?
- A. Immediately, as it reached the address
- **B. Just after midnight at the start of the following day, even though it is a weekend [correct]**
- C. At noon on the next working day, so Monday
- D. On the second working day after delivery
_Why:_ The policy states that a notice delivered by hand to the patient's usual or last known address is deemed served at the start of the following day, so a notice delivered at noon is served immediately after midnight even if it is a weekend or bank holiday. This matters because the patient is only AWOL from the point the notice is deemed served.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Community Treatment Order Policy MHA 1983 (June 2024, issue 06, review June 2027)

**Q244. [Hard]** _Scenario: A recall notice names one hospital, but by the time a bed is found it is on a different unit._ What does the trust's Community Treatment Order (CTO) policy require in this situation?
- A. The recall stands and the Mental Health Act Office is told retrospectively
- **B. A new Form CTO3 must be completed naming the hospital the patient is actually returned to [correct]**
- C. Form CTO6 is completed to authorise the change of hospital
- D. Verbal agreement with the bed flow co-ordinator is sufficient
_Why:_ The policy states that if a bed is identified in any hospital other than the one named on the recall notice a new CTO3 must be completed, because recall will be unlawful if the hospital on the notice is not the one the patient is returned to, leaving no authority for conveyance or detention.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Community Treatment Order Policy MHA 1983 (June 2024, issue 06, review June 2027)

**Q245. [Medium]** _Scenario: A recalled patient remains too unwell to return home and the team is considering revoking the Community Treatment Order._ By when must the assessment be done and Form CTO5 completed, according to trust policy?
- A. Within 24 hours of the patient arriving
- **B. During the 72-hour recall period [correct]**
- C. Within 7 days of admission
- D. Before the next multidisciplinary team (MDT) meeting
_Why:_ The policy states the patient must be assessed and the CTO5 revoke form completed during the 72-hour recall period, and that if more than 72 hours have passed since recall the CTO cannot be revoked. Revocation also needs the agreement of an Approved Mental Health Professional (AMHP).
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Community Treatment Order Policy MHA 1983 (June 2024, issue 06, review June 2027)

**Q246. [Hard]** _Scenario: A patient is about to be discharged from your ward onto a Community Treatment Order._ What does the trust policy expect ward staff to make sure happens around review by the Community Responsible Clinician (RC)?
- **A. The patient is seen by the Community RC before discharge, or an outpatient appointment is booked within one month of discharge onto the CTO [correct]**
- B. The patient is seen by the Community RC within 72 hours of discharge
- C. An outpatient appointment is booked within three months of discharge
- D. The care co-ordinator visits within 7 days and the RC reviews at the next renewal
_Why:_ The policy places this duty on ward staff so that the Community RC can review treatment and either complete a CTO12 or request a Second Opinion Appointed Doctor (SOAD) from the Care Quality Commission. Section 132A rights must also be explained and the completed form sent to the Mental Health Act Office within one month of discharge.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Community Treatment Order Policy MHA 1983 (June 2024, issue 06, review June 2027)

**Q247. [Medium]** Under the trust's Community Treatment Order (CTO) policy, how must the Responsible Clinician (RC) examine the patient when renewing a CTO?
- A. By video call where the patient is well known to the team
- **B. Face to face, with no exceptions [correct]**
- C. By telephone if the care co-ordinator has seen the patient recently
- D. Face to face only where the Approved Mental Health Professional (AMHP) asks for it
_Why:_ The policy states that CTO renewals must be conducted face to face and that there are no exceptions to this, following the case law Derbyshire Healthcare v Secretary of State for Health and Social Care. The RC must examine the patient and decide whether the criteria for extension are met.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Community Treatment Order Policy MHA 1983 (June 2024, issue 06, review June 2027)

**Q248. [Easy]** A Responsible Clinician (RC) wants to alter or suspend the conditions of a Community Treatment Order (CTO). Which trust form is used?
- A. Form CTO1
- **B. Form CTO2 [correct]**
- C. Form CTO7
- D. Form CTO12
_Why:_ The policy says the RC may alter or suspend the conditions by completing Form CTO2, which is returned to the Mental Health Act Office, who then write to the patient with the changes and a copy of the form. CTO1 makes the order and CTO7 extends it.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Community Treatment Order Policy MHA 1983 (June 2024, issue 06, review June 2027)

**Q249. [Hard]** _Scenario: A Community Treatment Order is due to expire and a managers review hearing will be needed._ How far ahead of the section expiry date should the completed CTO7 plus the medical and social circumstances reports reach the Mental Health Act Administrator (MHAA)?
- A. At least 1 week before
- B. At least 2 weeks before
- **C. At least 4 weeks before [correct]**
- D. At least 8 weeks before
_Why:_ The policy says a managers review hearing should be held before the section expiry date, so the completed CTO7 and reports should be received by the MHAA at least 4 weeks beforehand. The Mental Health Act Office also notifies the RC of the two-month expiry date.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Community Treatment Order Policy MHA 1983 (June 2024, issue 06, review June 2027)

**Q250. [Easy]** _Scenario: A detained patient on your ward is entitled to section 117 aftercare and discharge is being planned._ Under the joint section 117 policy, who arranges the aftercare planning meeting?
- A. The Integrated Care Board
- **B. Ward staff [correct]**
- C. The patient's solicitor
- D. The Mental Health Act Office
_Why:_ The policy states plainly that this meeting will be arranged by ward staff, and that thought must be given to how the person can best be supported to take part, for example easy read material, extra preparation time or advocacy.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Section 117 (after-care policy): Joint Policy, Procedure and Practice Guidance for After-care under s.117 Mental Health Act 1983 (Jan 2026, issue 7, review Dec 2026)

**Q251. [Hard]** Under the joint section 117 policy, how far before the expected discharge date should the pre-discharge planning meeting be held as a minimum?
- A. 1 week
- B. 2 weeks
- **C. 4 weeks [correct]**
- D. 6 weeks
_Why:_ The policy sets a minimum of 4 weeks before the expected discharge date, and sooner if the plan is likely to involve aftercare elements that will take longer than four weeks to finalise. The meeting must be clearly recorded as a section 117 pre-discharge meeting.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Section 117 (after-care policy): Joint Policy, Procedure and Practice Guidance for After-care under s.117 Mental Health Act 1983 (Jan 2026, issue 7, review Dec 2026)

**Q252. [Medium]** _Scenario: You are checking the invite list for a section 117 aftercare planning meeting._ Which of these does the joint section 117 policy list as a must-attend at that meeting?
- **A. The Ward Named Nurse [correct]**
- B. The Mental Health Act Administrator
- C. The patient's GP
- D. The ward manager
_Why:_ The policy's must-attend list is the person entitled to aftercare and/or their carer or representative, the Responsible Clinician, a representative from Social Care, the Ward Named Nurse, and community named professionals as appropriate. An Independent Mental Health Advocate (IMHA) should be invited, and if an IMHA is not used the reasons must be documented in the case notes.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Section 117 (after-care policy): Joint Policy, Procedure and Practice Guidance for After-care under s.117 Mental Health Act 1983 (Jan 2026, issue 7, review Dec 2026)

**Q253. [Hard]** _Scenario: The section 117 aftercare planning meeting has finished and the plan has been agreed._ Under the joint policy, who completes and circulates the care plan, and within what time?
- A. The Ward Named Nurse, within 5 working days
- **B. The Care Co-ordinator, within 10 working days of the meeting [correct]**
- C. The Responsible Clinician, within 14 days
- D. Social Care, before the patient leaves the ward
_Why:_ The policy makes the Care Co-ordinator responsible for completion and circulation of the care plan within 10 working days of the meeting. A copy of the Discharge Care Plan must then be attached by the social worker to the 'Form A' Agreement of Section 117 Entitlement.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Section 117 (after-care policy): Joint Policy, Procedure and Practice Guidance for After-care under s.117 Mental Health Act 1983 (Jan 2026, issue 7, review Dec 2026)

**Q254. [Medium]** _Scenario: A patient has just been placed on section 3 on your ward._ Under the joint section 117 policy, who adds the marker to the electronic patient record showing the person is now section 117 entitled?
- A. The Mental Health Act Administrator
- B. The Approved Mental Health Professional who made the application
- **C. The named nurse for an inpatient, or the care co-ordinator for a community patient [correct]**
- D. The Integrated Care Board's continuing care team
_Why:_ The policy says the appropriate healthcare professional, usually the named nurse if an inpatient or the care co-ordinator if a community patient, adds a marker to the electronic patient record that the person is now section 117 entitled. Local Authority systems separately record the legal status using the Advanced Mental Health Professional monitoring form.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Section 117 (after-care policy): Joint Policy, Procedure and Practice Guidance for After-care under s.117 Mental Health Act 1983 (Jan 2026, issue 7, review Dec 2026)

**Q255. [Easy]** _Scenario: A review has concluded that a person no longer needs section 117 aftercare._ Under the joint policy, what must happen for entitlement to be validly ended?
- A. The Responsible Clinician records the decision in the electronic patient record
- **B. Form B, discharge from section 117, is completed and signed by representatives of both Health and Social Services [correct]**
- C. The care co-ordinator writes to the person and closes the case
- D. The Integrated Care Board authorises the end of funding
_Why:_ The policy requires the decision to be taken at a review and recorded on Form B, signed by representatives of Health and Social Services, with copies to the Mental Health Act Manager at the relevant Trust and the Finance Manager at the relevant Local Authority. Ending entitlement is a joint decision and no single agency can make it alone.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Section 117 (after-care policy): Joint Policy, Procedure and Practice Guidance for After-care under s.117 Mental Health Act 1983 (Jan 2026, issue 7, review Dec 2026)

**Q256. [Easy]** _Scenario: A Mental Health Tribunal has been listed for a detained patient on your ward._ Under trust policy, who should write the nursing report?
- A. The ward manager, as the accountable person
- B. The Mental Health Act Administrator
- **C. The named nurse, or a nurse with good knowledge of the patient [correct]**
- D. The Responsible Clinician, with a nursing countersignature
_Why:_ The policy says the nursing report describes the patient's presentation on the ward and should be produced by the named nurse or a nurse with good knowledge of the patient. It is required for inpatients only.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Mental Health Tribunal Policy and Procedures (September 2025, issue 04, review September 2028)

**Q257. [Medium]** Under the trust Mental Health Tribunal policy, within what period of the date of application must tribunal reports be submitted to the Mental Health Act Administrator (MHAA)?
- A. Within 1 week
- **B. Within 3 weeks [correct]**
- C. Within 6 weeks
- D. By 14 days before the hearing
_Why:_ The policy places a statutory duty on the Trust to provide the hospital statement, medical, social circumstances and nursing reports, and these must reach the MHAA within 3 weeks of the date of application so they can be sent on to the tribunal office in time. If reports are late the tribunal can issue an Order, and then a summons or a fine.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Mental Health Tribunal Policy and Procedures (September 2025, issue 04, review September 2028)

**Q258. [Hard]** _Scenario: A section 2 tribunal hearing is listed for Monday morning._ Under trust policy, when must the reports be in?
- **A. By 16:00 on the Wednesday before, as section 2 reports go to the tribunal 48 hours before the hearing [correct]**
- B. Three weeks before the hearing, as for all other sections
- C. By close of play on the Friday before
- D. On the morning of the hearing
_Why:_ The policy gives this exact worked example: section 2 reports must be submitted to the tribunal 48 hours before the hearing, so for a Monday morning hearing they are required by 16:00 on the Wednesday. Section 2 reports go to the dedicated section 2 email address on receipt.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Mental Health Tribunal Policy and Procedures (September 2025, issue 04, review September 2028)

**Q259. [Medium]** _Scenario: You are chasing the reports for a tribunal and want to know who is producing what._ Under trust policy, who produces the social circumstances report?
- A. The Mental Health Act team
- B. The Responsible Clinician
