_Why:_ The policy states treatment is given for five days, stopped for two days, then the patient is re-screened with a second screen a further 24 hours later. Two post treatment screens are needed, and treatment must not be repeated except on the advice of a Consultant Microbiologist.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - MRSA Policy and Procedure (August 2025, issue 08, review August 2028)

**Q428. [Medium]** _Scenario: The ward has one side room and staff are discussing whether an MRSA positive patient needs it._ Which situations does the trust's MRSA policy give as examples where a single room is required?
- A. Any patient who is MRSA colonised, in all circumstances
- B. Only patients who refuse decolonisation treatment
- C. Only patients who are also positive for Clostridioides difficile
- **D. An MRSA chest infection with a productive cough, a shedding skin condition such as psoriasis, or leaking wounds not contained by dressings [correct]**
_Why:_ The policy says a single bedroom is preferable for MRSA colonisation but the patient need not stay in the room unless isolated for one of these reasons. Any additional measures are agreed with the Infection Control Team, documented in a care plan, and the Head of Nursing for the area must be involved.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - MRSA Policy and Procedure (August 2025, issue 08, review August 2028)

**Q429. [Easy]** _Scenario: A colleague sustains a needlestick injury while giving an intramuscular injection._ What immediate first aid does the trust's Managing an Inoculation Incident policy require?
- A. Suck the wound to draw out blood, then apply a plaster
- **B. Wash the site liberally with soap and water without scrubbing, encourage free bleeding, dry the area and apply a waterproof dressing [correct]**
- C. Scrub the site hard with an alcohol wipe and apply a dry dressing
- D. Soak the finger in a chlorhexidine solution for five minutes
_Why:_ The policy's first aid steps are to wash liberally with soap and water without scrubbing, not to suck the wound, to encourage free bleeding of puncture wounds, and to dry and apply a waterproof dressing. Exposed mucous membranes are irrigated with water.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Managing an Inoculation Incident Policy and Procedure (August 2025, issue 6, review August 2028)

**Q430. [Easy]** Who must an inoculation incident be reported to first under the trust's policy?
- **A. Immediately to a senior member of staff, for example the nurse in charge or head of department, who makes an initial risk assessment of the incident [correct]**
- B. The Infection Control Team on the next working day
- C. The Trust Risk Management Department, by DATIX report only
- D. The injured staff member's own general practitioner (GP)
_Why:_ The policy requires all incidents to be reported immediately to a senior member of staff such as the nurse in charge, who carries out the initial risk assessment and ensures the required actions follow. An Untoward Incident Report form is also completed.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Managing an Inoculation Incident Policy and Procedure (August 2025, issue 6, review August 2028)

**Q431. [Hard]** What timings does the trust's policy give for HIV post exposure prophylaxis (PEP)?
- A. Start within 24 hours and take for 7 days
- B. Start within 6 hours and take for 14 days
- C. Start within 72 hours and take for 3 months
- **D. Supply and commence as soon as possible and ideally within one hour of exposure, never more than 72 hours after the incident, and continue for 28 days [correct]**
_Why:_ The policy states HIV PEP should be commenced as soon as possible and ideally within one hour, should not be commenced more than 72 hours after the incident, and needs to be taken for 28 days to be most effective.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Managing an Inoculation Incident Policy and Procedure (August 2025, issue 6, review August 2028)

**Q432. [Medium]** _Scenario: It is 9pm and a staff member sustains what looks like a high risk exposure._ What does the trust's policy say should happen out of hours?
- A. Leave a message on the Occupational Health answer machine and wait for a call back the next working day
- **B. The senior member of staff must contact the Emergency Department, and if HIV exposure is suspected the injured worker may be asked to attend for treatment which should start within one hour [correct]**
- C. Wait until Occupational Health opens the next working morning
- D. Ask the ward pharmacist to dispense post exposure prophylaxis (PEP) from ward stock
_Why:_ For high risk incidents the policy says to contact Occupational Health without delay in working hours, and outside those times the senior member of staff must contact the Emergency Department. The answer machine route applies only to low risk incidents involving high risk fluids.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Managing an Inoculation Incident Policy and Procedure (August 2025, issue 6, review August 2028)

**Q433. [Easy]** _Scenario: The sharps bin in the clinic room is filling up._ When must a sharps container be sealed and disposed of under trust policy?
- A. Only when it is completely full to the top
- B. At the end of every shift
- **C. When three quarters full or at the indicator line, or at maximum intervals not exceeding three months, whichever is earlier [correct]**
- D. Every week without exception
_Why:_ Trust policy requires disposal at three quarters full or the indicator line, or at intervals not exceeding three months, whichever comes first. Containers must be labelled with the date started, ward or department and the initials of the person assembling them, and sealed containers go directly into a lockable clinical waste container, not a yellow bag.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Managing an Inoculation Incident Policy and Procedure (August 2025, issue 6, review August 2028)

**Q434. [Hard]** _Scenario: There is a blood spillage on the hard floor of the clinic room._ How does the trust's Cleaning, Disinfection and Decontamination policy say this should be dealt with?
- A. Wipe up with warm water and general purpose detergent only
- B. Apply alcohol gel to the spillage and leave it to dry
- **C. Wearing gloves and apron, cover with paper towels and apply a chlorine releasing disinfectant at 10,000 parts per million for a minimum of 3 minutes, remove the residual waste into an orange waste bag, then clean the area with warm water and general purpose detergent [correct]**
- D. Apply a 1,000 parts per million chlorine solution for 30 seconds then rinse
_Why:_ The policy specifies a chlorine releasing disinfectant at 10,000 parts per million with a minimum 3 minute contact time for blood spillages, residual waste removed with disposable paper into an orange waste bag, and only then cleaning with warm water and general purpose detergent.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Cleaning, Disinfection and Decontamination Clinical Policy and Procedure (May 2025, issue 7, review May 2028)

**Q435. [Medium]** _Scenario: A patient has spilled urine on the floor of the day area._ What does the trust's cleaning policy specifically warn against here?
- A. Using paper towels to absorb the spill
- B. Wearing gloves and an apron, as these are not needed for urine
- C. Using warm water and general purpose detergent to remove the organic material
- **D. Applying a chlorine releasing agent such as Actichlor directly onto urine, as this may result in rapid release of toxic levels of chlorine [correct]**
_Why:_ The policy is explicit that chlorine releasing agents must not be applied directly onto urine. Organic material is removed first with warm water and general purpose detergent, and no further decontamination is needed for urine unless visible blood is present.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Cleaning, Disinfection and Decontamination Clinical Policy and Procedure (May 2025, issue 7, review May 2028)

**Q436. [Easy]** How often must curtains in clinical and ward areas be changed under the trust's cleaning policy?
- A. Once a year
- **B. A minimum of every six months, with weekly checks, and taken down and laundered immediately if splashed with body fluids [correct]**
- C. Every two years
- D. Only when they are visibly dirty
_Why:_ The cleaning schedule in the policy sets curtains at a minimum of every six months with weekly checks, and immediate laundering if splashed with body fluids. Disposable curtains are used after risk assessment, clearly labelled and changed after six months or if contaminated.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Cleaning, Disinfection and Decontamination Clinical Policy and Procedure (May 2025, issue 7, review May 2028)

**Q437. [Hard]** What does the trust's mattress care and decontamination guidance require ward staff to do each month?
- A. Send every mattress away for external decontamination
- B. Replace all mattress covers
- C. Steam clean every mattress on the ward
- **D. Turn the mattress as per the manufacturer's instructions, open the zip and visually check the foam for staining, condemning and replacing any stained mattress as soon as reasonably practicable, with a record held at ward level [correct]**
_Why:_ The mattress appendix requires a monthly turn with the zip opened and the foam checked for stains. Stained mattresses, or any with splits or breaks in the covering, must be condemned and replaced, and a local record of actions and of the age of the mattress kept at ward level. Fully sealed mattresses need only an external visual inspection.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Cleaning, Disinfection and Decontamination Clinical Policy and Procedure (May 2025, issue 7, review May 2028)

**Q438. [Hard]** _Scenario: A doctor confirms scabies in a patient on the ward._ Which contacts does the trust's scabies guideline say should be treated?
- A. Everyone who has set foot on the ward in the past month, regardless of contact
- B. Only the patient's named nurse
- **C. Other inpatients, ward staff, other healthcare workers, family members or regular visitors who have had prolonged contact, defined as at least 5 minutes constant skin to skin contact [correct]**
- D. Only people who have already developed a rash or itching
_Why:_ The guideline defines prolonged contact as at least 5 minutes constant skin to skin contact and says those contacts should be treated. Family members and visitors are advised to request treatment from their own GP.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Scabies Clinical Guideline and Procedures (April 2023, issue 1, review May 2028)

**Q439. [Easy]** Under the trust's scabies guideline, how many treatments should the affected patient and their contacts receive?
- **A. The infected patient is treated twice, 7 days apart, and contacts receive one precautionary treatment [correct]**
- B. Both the patient and their contacts receive a single treatment
- C. The patient is treated three times on consecutive days and contacts are not treated
- D. The patient is treated twice, 24 hours apart, and contacts are treated weekly for a month
_Why:_ The guideline states treatment should be prescribed and undertaken twice, 7 days apart, and that contacts are advised to receive one treatment which is precautionary in intent. Inpatients are prescribed treatment as a stat dose on the Medication Prescription and Administration chart.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Scabies Clinical Guideline and Procedures (April 2023, issue 1, review May 2028)

**Q440. [Medium]** _Scenario: Scabies has been confirmed in a clinical area and several staff have had close contact._ Who contacts the Infection Control Team (ICT), and who authorises treatment for affected staff?
- A. The ward pharmacist contacts the ICT, and the on call doctor authorises staff treatment
- **B. The Appointed Practitioner in Charge contacts the ICT for advice, and Occupational Health authorises treatment for staff and confirms the product to be used [correct]**
- C. The Domestic Supervisor contacts the ICT, and the patient's consultant authorises staff treatment
- D. Any staff member contacts the UK Health Security Agency, who authorise treatment
_Why:_ The guideline says the Appointed Practitioner in Charge contacts the ICT for advice, and the ICT contacts Occupational Health, who authorise staff treatment and confirm the product. If the ICT is unavailable, contact Occupational Health directly and inform the Senior Nurse, Head of Nursing or Area Service Manager.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Scabies Clinical Guideline and Procedures (April 2023, issue 1, review May 2028)

**Q441. [Easy]** The trust uses the nationally agreed colour coding for disposable aprons and cleaning equipment. Which colour is used when nursing a patient in isolation or cohort nursing during an outbreak?
- A. Red
- B. Green
- **C. Yellow [correct]**
- D. Blue
_Why:_ In the trust's colour coding table yellow is for nursing a patient in isolation or cohort nursing in an outbreak. Red is for bathrooms and assisting with toileting, blue for general areas and routine personal care, green for catering and food, and white or clear for clean procedures such as wound care and catheterisation.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Standard Infection Control Principles Policy and Procedure (05 November 2025, issue 07, review November 2028)

**Q442. [Easy]** Which glove type does the trust's Standard Infection Control Principles policy specify for routine clinical use as personal protective equipment (PPE)?
- **A. Powder free nitrile, non latex [correct]**
- B. Polythene or vinyl
- C. Sterile powder free latex surgeons' gloves
- D. Whatever is available, since glove type does not matter for routine care
_Why:_ The policy's glove selection table gives powder free nitrile (non latex) for routine clinical use, states latex free gloves must be used and latex gloves should not be in use, and says vinyl or polythene gloves are not recommended for clinical practice.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Standard Infection Control Principles Policy and Procedure (05 November 2025, issue 07, review November 2028)

**Q443. [Hard]** _Scenario: A patient known to be colonised with MRSA dies on the ward and staff ask whether a body bag is needed._ What does the trust's Standard Infection Control Principles policy say?
- A. A body bag is always required for any patient with MRSA
- **B. The presence of MRSA or Clostridioides difficile infection or colonisation does not require the routine use of a body bag [correct]**
- C. A body bag is required and relatives cannot view the body
- D. A body bag is used only if the family request one
_Why:_ The policy is explicit that MRSA and Clostridium difficile infection or colonisation do not require routine use of a body bag, and warns that inappropriate use may cause unnecessary distress to relatives. A notification form must still accompany the body to advise of any special precautions or infection risk.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Standard Infection Control Principles Policy and Procedure (05 November 2025, issue 07, review November 2028)

**Q444. [Easy]** _Scenario: The alcohol hand rub dispenser by the ward door is nearly empty and there is a part used bottle of the same product in the store cupboard._ What does the trust's Hand Hygiene policy say about this?
- **A. Never top up bottles or dispensers; they should be regarded as single use, and dispensers that require topping up must not be installed in Trust premises without specific clearance from Infection Control [correct]**
- B. Top it up, provided the same product and batch is used
- C. Top it up and record the batch number in the ward cleaning file
- D. Top it up only if the spare bottle was opened less than 24 hours ago
_Why:_ The policy states topping up of bottles should never occur because the inside of bottles, even those with antiseptic solutions, can become breeding grounds for bacteria, and dispensers should be regarded as single use.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Hand Hygiene Policy and Procedure (May 2025, issue 6, review May 2028)

## Trust Policy - Substance Misuse & Smoking

**Q445. [Easy]** _Scenario: A patient is admitted overnight and tells you they drink heavily most days._ Which validated screening tool does the trust guideline name for screening all patients for alcohol misuse on admission?
- A. The Severity of Alcohol Dependence Questionnaire (SADQ)
- **B. AUDIT-C [correct]**
- C. The Clinical Institute Withdrawal Assessment (CIWA)
- D. The Fagerstrom Test for Nicotine Dependence (FTND)
_Why:_ The trust guideline states that all patients admitted to a psychiatric setting should be screened for alcohol misuse using a validated screening tool such as AUDIT-C or FAST, and lists AUDIT-C screening on admission under what is expected of nurses. SADQ and CIWA come later, to assess dependence severity and monitor withdrawal.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Alcohol Withdrawal in Inpatient Psychiatric Settings: Management of Acute Withdrawal Clinical Guideline and Policy (March 2026, issue 07, review November 2027)

**Q446. [Hard]** AUDIT-C gives a total score out of 12. According to the trust alcohol withdrawal guideline, which total suggests potential alcohol dependence?
- A. A score of 5 or more
- B. A score of 8 or more
- **C. A score of 11 or 12 [correct]**
- D. Any score above 0
_Why:_ The guideline says AUDIT-C gives a total out of 12, that a score of 11 or 12 suggests potential alcohol dependence, and that a score of 5 or more indicates alcohol excess. The two thresholds are easy to mix up.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Alcohol Withdrawal in Inpatient Psychiatric Settings: Management of Acute Withdrawal Clinical Guideline and Policy (March 2026, issue 07, review November 2027)

**Q447. [Medium]** _Scenario: A patient is on a reducing chlordiazepoxide regime and the lunchtime dose is due._ Under the trust guideline, what must nursing staff do before administering each dose?
- A. Repeat the Severity of Alcohol Dependence Questionnaire (SADQ)
- B. Take a breath alcohol reading with the alcoholmeter
- **C. Undertake monitoring of withdrawal signs using the Clinical Institute Withdrawal Assessment (CIWA) [correct]**
- D. Ask the doctor to review the patient in person
_Why:_ The nursing expectations in the guideline state that staff must undertake monitoring of withdrawal signs before administering each dose, using CIWA. SADQ is used once at baseline to assess severity of dependence and guide the regimen.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Alcohol Withdrawal in Inpatient Psychiatric Settings: Management of Acute Withdrawal Clinical Guideline and Policy (March 2026, issue 07, review November 2027)

**Q448. [Easy]** For a patient being treated for acute alcohol withdrawal, what does the trust guideline set as the minimum frequency of medical review?
- **A. Daily [correct]**
- B. Every 48 hours
- C. Twice weekly
- D. Only if the Clinical Institute Withdrawal Assessment (CIWA) score rises
_Why:_ The guideline lists 'ensure daily medical review as a minimum' among the nursing expectations, and requires doctors to review withdrawal symptoms daily and adjust the titration regimen and when-required prescription accordingly.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Alcohol Withdrawal in Inpatient Psychiatric Settings: Management of Acute Withdrawal Clinical Guideline and Policy (March 2026, issue 07, review November 2027)

**Q449. [Hard]** _Scenario: An alcohol dependent patient arrives on the ward at 2am and is still visibly intoxicated. Detoxification is being planned._ What does the trust guideline say about the timing of prophylactic parenteral thiamine?
- A. It should be withheld until the patient is no longer intoxicated
- **B. It should be given as soon as practicable, within 12 hours of admission, irrespective of whether the patient is still intoxicated [correct]**
- C. It should be started once the Clinical Institute Withdrawal Assessment (CIWA) score reaches a moderate level
- D. It should be given within 72 hours of admission once bloods are back
_Why:_ The guideline states that all patients commenced on alcohol detoxification should be prophylactically treated with parenteral thiamine as soon as practicable, within 12 hours of admission, irrespective of whether the patient is still intoxicated, because of the high risk of Wernicke's encephalopathy.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Alcohol Withdrawal in Inpatient Psychiatric Settings: Management of Acute Withdrawal Clinical Guideline and Policy (March 2026, issue 07, review November 2027)

**Q450. [Hard]** What prophylactic thiamine regimen for Wernicke's encephalopathy does the trust flowchart specify for a patient starting alcohol detoxification?
- A. 100mg thiamine orally three times a day for 5 days
- **B. 250mg thiamine intramuscularly daily for 3 days [correct]**
- C. 500mg thiamine intravenously three times a day for 2 days
- D. 300mg thiamine intramuscularly as a single dose
_Why:_ The trust flowchart and thiamine section both specify 250mg thiamine intramuscularly daily for 3 days as prophylactic treatment, with the first dose given as soon as practical. Intravenous thiamine is reserved for presumed or diagnosed Wernicke's encephalopathy, which requires urgent transfer to an acute setting.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Alcohol Withdrawal in Inpatient Psychiatric Settings: Management of Acute Withdrawal Clinical Guideline and Policy (March 2026, issue 07, review November 2027)

**Q451. [Hard]** _Scenario: A patient has completed three days of intramuscular thiamine and is eating and drinking normally._ According to the trust guideline, what happens next with thiamine?
- A. Thiamine is stopped once the intramuscular course is finished
- **B. Oral thiamine 50mg four times daily is started and, following successful withdrawal, continued for six weeks [correct]**
- C. Oral thiamine 100mg once daily is started and continued for two weeks
- D. Vitamin B compound strong is substituted for thiamine
_Why:_ The guideline states that if the patient can manage oral therapy after three days of intramuscular thiamine, oral thiamine should be started at 50mg four times daily, and that following successful alcohol withdrawal thiamine should be continued for six weeks before being reviewed for discontinuation.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Alcohol Withdrawal in Inpatient Psychiatric Settings: Management of Acute Withdrawal Clinical Guideline and Policy (March 2026, issue 07, review November 2027)

**Q452. [Medium]** What does the trust alcohol withdrawal guideline say about Vitamin B compound and Vitamin B compound strong?
- A. They are the first line choice for thiamine replacement on the ward
- B. They may be used where intramuscular thiamine is refused
- **C. They are not recommended and should not be prescribed [correct]**
- D. They should be prescribed alongside thiamine for the first six weeks
_Why:_ The guideline is explicit that these preparations are not recommended and should not be prescribed, referring to the Derbyshire Joint Area Prescribing Committee (JAPC) position statement.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Alcohol Withdrawal in Inpatient Psychiatric Settings: Management of Acute Withdrawal Clinical Guideline and Policy (March 2026, issue 07, review November 2027)

**Q453. [Medium]** _Scenario: A patient starting a chlordiazepoxide regime is already prescribed a regular benzodiazepine._ How does the trust guideline say chlordiazepoxide should be prescribed in this situation?
- A. The existing benzodiazepine should be stopped first and replaced by chlordiazepoxide
- **B. Chlordiazepoxide should be prescribed 'on top' of any benzodiazepines the patient is already taking [correct]**
- C. The existing benzodiazepine dose should be halved and chlordiazepoxide added
- D. Chlordiazepoxide should not be used at all if a benzodiazepine is already prescribed
_Why:_ The guideline states directly that chlordiazepoxide should be prescribed 'on top' of any benzodiazepines the patient is already taking. Existing benzodiazepine use is one of the individual risk factors prescribers are told to take into account.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Alcohol Withdrawal in Inpatient Psychiatric Settings: Management of Acute Withdrawal Clinical Guideline and Policy (March 2026, issue 07, review November 2027)

**Q454. [Medium]** _Scenario: A patient on day 3 of a chlordiazepoxide reduction is being discharged, and also asks for leave over the weekend._ What does the trust guideline require regarding the withdrawal benzodiazepine?
- A. The remaining regime should be dispensed as a take home supply to complete at home
- B. The regime should be converted to a when-required prescription for the patient to self-manage
- **C. Benzodiazepines for withdrawal should not be continued on leave or discharge, and the variable and when-required doses should be stopped [correct]**
- D. The general practitioner should be asked to continue and extend the regime in the community
_Why:_ The trust flowchart carries the instruction 'do not continue benzodiazepines for withdrawal on leave or discharge', and the text states that if the patient is discharged before completion of the regimen the benzodiazepine variable and when-required doses used for managing withdrawal should be stopped.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Alcohol Withdrawal in Inpatient Psychiatric Settings: Management of Acute Withdrawal Clinical Guideline and Policy (March 2026, issue 07, review November 2027)

**Q455. [Hard]** On the trust's Severity of Alcohol Dependence Questionnaire (SADQ) form, which score range is labelled as severe dependence?
- A. 4 to 19
- B. 20 to 30
- **C. 31 to 44 [correct]**
- D. 45 and above
_Why:_ The trust SADQ form scores 0 to 3 as no dependence, 4 to 19 mild, 20 to 30 moderate, 31 to 44 severe and 45 or more very severe. The withdrawal guideline's suggested regimens are built on the same bands, with the moderate regimen for SADQ 20 to 30 and the severe regimen for SADQ 31 to 44.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Severity of Alcohol Dependence Questionnaire (SADQ) (Issue 01, adopted by the Medicines Management Committee August 2017)

**Q456. [Hard]** _Scenario: You are completing the Clinical Institute Withdrawal Assessment - Alcohol (CIWA-A) form with a patient._ Most CIWA-A items are rated 0 to 7. Which item on the trust form is rated on a different scale?
- **A. Orientation and clouding of sensorium, rated 0 to 4 [correct]**
- B. Paroxysmal sweats, rated 0 to 10
- C. Tremor, rated 0 to 3
- D. Headache, rated 0 to 5
_Why:_ On the trust CIWA-A scoring guidelines every item is rated 0 to 7 except orientation and clouding of sensorium, which is rated on a 0 to 4 scale. Getting this wrong inflates the total score.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Clinical Institute Withdrawal Assessment - Alcohol (CIWA-A) (Issue 01, adopted by the Medicines Management Committee August 2017)

**Q457. [Hard]** _Scenario: A newly admitted patient discloses regular use of alcohol and street drugs._ Which assessment documentation does the trust Intoxication Management Policy name for the more in-depth substance use assessment?
- A. The AUDIT-C form on the SystmOne launchpad
- **B. The SUFARI assessment documentation and guidance within the SystmOne electronic patient record [correct]**
- C. The Severity of Alcohol Dependence Questionnaire (SADQ) on paper
- D. The Fagerstrom Test for Nicotine Dependence (FTND)
_Why:_ The policy states that all individuals admitted to an inpatient unit will be asked about alcohol and substance use and, if indicated, a more in-depth assessment made, using the SUFARI assessment documentation and guidance within the SystmOne electronic patient record.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Intoxication Management Policy and Procedure (April 2023, issue 3, review February 2026)
_⚠ review date passed_

**Q458. [Easy]** _Scenario: A patient arrives for admission smelling strongly of alcohol and a colleague suggests turning them away until they are sober._ What does the trust Intoxication Management Policy say about this?
- **A. A decision not to admit must not be based on the use of alcohol or illicit drugs; the decision to admit is based on the overall clinical assessment [correct]**
- B. Admission must be deferred until a breath alcohol reading of zero is obtained
- C. Admission can be refused if the patient is intoxicated with alcohol but not if intoxicated with drugs
- D. Admission should be delayed for four hours and then reconsidered
_Why:_ The policy states plainly that a decision not to admit must not be based on the use of alcohol or illicit drugs, and that the decision to admit will be based on the clinical assessment of the overall presenting clinical picture.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Intoxication Management Policy and Procedure (April 2023, issue 3, review February 2026)
_⚠ review date passed_

**Q459. [Hard]** _Scenario: A patient returns to the ward having been drinking and agrees to use the alcoholmeter._ How long does the trust policy say should have elapsed since the last drink before using the alcoholmeter?
- A. At least 5 minutes
- **B. At least 20 minutes [correct]**
- C. At least 60 minutes
- D. There is no waiting period
_Why:_ The policy requires a period of at least twenty minutes since the patient's last drink before using the alcoholmeter, because residual alcohol in the mouth can give a falsely high reading.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Intoxication Management Policy and Procedure (April 2023, issue 3, review February 2026)
_⚠ review date passed_

**Q460. [Hard]** Once alcoholmeter monitoring has started, how often does the trust policy say it should be repeated and when can it stop?
- A. Every 15 minutes until the reading is zero
- **B. Every 30 minutes until two consecutive readings have shown a reduction [correct]**
- C. Hourly until the reading falls below the drink driving level
- D. Once on arrival and once again at handover
_Why:_ The policy states the alcoholmeter should be administered every 30 minutes until such time as two consecutive readings have shown a reduction. It also warns the reading confirms alcohol is present, not the level of intoxication.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Intoxication Management Policy and Procedure (April 2023, issue 3, review February 2026)
_⚠ review date passed_

**Q461. [Medium]** _Scenario: An intoxicated patient has settled in their room and physical observations are being planned._ What is the minimum monitoring frequency set out in the trust Intoxication Management Policy?
- A. Hourly throughout, then four hourly overnight
- B. Continuous within eyesight observation until the patient is fully sober
- **C. Half hourly initially, then hourly until clinical judgement confirms the level of intoxication has reduced to safe levels [correct]**
- D. Two hourly, increasing only if the National Early Warning Score (NEWS2) rises
_Why:_ The policy says monitoring requirements are informed by the individual's presenting condition but as a minimum must be carried out half hourly initially and then hourly, until clinical judgement, aided by the alcoholmeter where alcohol is involved, confirms intoxication has reduced to safe levels.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Intoxication Management Policy and Procedure (April 2023, issue 3, review February 2026)
_⚠ review date passed_

**Q462. [Easy]** _Scenario: An intoxicated patient's regular evening antipsychotic is due._ What does the trust Intoxication Management Policy say about prescribed medication while a patient is intoxicated?
- A. Give the dose as normal but observe more closely afterwards
- B. Give half the prescribed dose
- **C. Omit prescribed medication until the next dose or until the patient is not intoxicated, unless there are potential contraindications for doing so [correct]**
- D. Omit all medication for the following 24 hours
_Why:_ The policy states that if the patient is intoxicated with illicit drugs or alcohol then prescribed medication should be omitted until the next dose or until the patient is not intoxicated, unless there are potential contraindications, and that medical or on-call pharmacy advice can be sought.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Intoxication Management Policy and Procedure (April 2023, issue 3, review February 2026)
_⚠ review date passed_

**Q463. [Hard]** _Scenario: A patient who had previously agreed with the team not to use alcohol or drugs during admission returns to the ward intoxicated._ What does the trust policy require in addition to completing an incident form?
- **A. A multidisciplinary review must be held within the next 24 hours to decide future management plans [correct]**
- B. The patient must be reviewed at the next scheduled ward round
- C. A safeguarding referral must be made within 72 hours
- D. The consultant must be contacted immediately at home
_Why:_ The documentation and post-incident section states that where a prior agreement had been made with the service user about not using alcohol or illicit drugs as an inpatient, a multidisciplinary review must be held within the next 24 hours to decide future management plans.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Intoxication Management Policy and Procedure (April 2023, issue 3, review February 2026)
_⚠ review date passed_

**Q464. [Easy]** _Scenario: A patient is known to both mental health services and a substance misuse service._ What does the trust co-occurring disorders policy say about using either problem as a reason to exclude someone from services?
- A. Substance misuse can be a reason to exclude from specialist mental health services if it is the dominant problem
- **B. Neither a mental health diagnosis nor substance misuse will be criteria for exclusion from specialist or generic mental health services [correct]**
- C. Patients must be abstinent before mental health services will accept a referral
- D. Only patients open to the Community Mental Health Team can be accepted
_Why:_ The policy states that neither mental health diagnosis nor substance misuse will be criteria for exclusion from the trust's specialist or generic mental health services. This is the local expression of the 'no wrong door' principle it references.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Management of People with Co-occurring Disorders or Co-occurring Mental Health and Substance Misuse Issues Policy and Procedure (Nov 2023, issue 6, review November 2026)

**Q465. [Medium]** _Scenario: Someone with both mental health and drug problems first presents to the substance misuse service, and there is uncertainty about who should pick the case up._ What does the trust co-occurring disorders policy say should happen?
- A. The case is passed straight to mental health services because they always lead
- **B. The service the client is first in contact with holds the case until a full assessment has been completed, after which the lead agency is agreed and a letter sent to the referrer [correct]**
- C. Both services open the case and work in parallel with separate care plans
- D. The case is held by whichever service has capacity that week
_Why:_ The policy states it is the responsibility of the service the client is first in contact with to hold the case until a full assessment has been completed, after which there should be agreement on the lead agency with clear roles and responsibilities on a treatment plan, and a letter sent to the referrer.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Management of People with Co-occurring Disorders or Co-occurring Mental Health and Substance Misuse Issues Policy and Procedure (Nov 2023, issue 6, review November 2026)

**Q466. [Medium]** _Scenario: An inpatient has a severe and enduring mental illness alongside problematic alcohol use._ Under the trust co-occurring disorders policy, who holds responsibility for managing this case?
- A. The substance misuse service, with mental health services advising
- **B. The mental health service, assisted by staff from substance misuse services if required [correct]**
- C. Whichever service the patient prefers
- D. Primary care, with both specialist services offering advice
_Why:_ The policy states that where there is severe mental illness with problematic substance misuse, management is the responsibility of the mental health service assisted by substance misuse staff if required. The reverse applies where the presenting problem is substance use and mental health difficulties are minimal, when a substance misuse worker case manages with access to a named mental health worker.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Management of People with Co-occurring Disorders or Co-occurring Mental Health and Substance Misuse Issues Policy and Procedure (Nov 2023, issue 6, review November 2026)

**Q467. [Medium]** Who does the trust co-occurring disorders policy say must complete co-occurring mental health and substance misuse (COMHAD) training?
- A. Only registered nurses working in substance misuse services
- B. Only medical staff and care coordinators
- **C. All clinical staff, including registered and support staff, and administrative staff where they have face to face contact with service users [correct]**
- D. Staff who request it as part of their personal development plan
_Why:_ The policy makes COMHAD training mandatory for all clinical staff including registered and support staff, and administrative staff where they have face to face contact with service users, so the workforce is competent and confident in working with people who have a co-existing diagnosis.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Management of People with Co-occurring Disorders or Co-occurring Mental Health and Substance Misuse Issues Policy and Procedure (Nov 2023, issue 6, review November 2026)

**Q468. [Hard]** _Scenario: A patient is admitted with cigarettes, a lighter and a pouch of tobacco._ What does the trust Smoke Free Sites Policy say should happen to these items?
- **A. They should be collected on arrival and handed to a family member or trusted friend within 48 hours, or otherwise disposed of [correct]**
- B. They should be placed in the patient's property store and returned on discharge
- C. They should be kept in the clinic room and returned for use during Section 17 leave
- D. The patient may keep them provided they agree not to smoke on site
_Why:_ The policy states that tobacco products, cigarettes, lighters and matches should be collected on arrival and handed to a family member or trusted friend within 48 hours or alternatively disposed of, and that tobacco products are not to be stored and returned during a patient's stay.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Smoke Free Sites Policy (May 2025, issue 1, review January 2028)

**Q469. [Easy]** _Scenario: A detained patient asks for escorted Section 17 leave so they can have a cigarette off site._ What does the trust Smoke Free Sites Policy say?
- A. Escorted leave for smoking is acceptable if it prevents an incident
- B. Leave for smoking can be granted once daily at the nurse in charge's discretion
- **C. Staff on inpatient wards should not facilitate or accompany patients off site to smoke, and this includes using Section 17 leave to facilitate smoking [correct]**
- D. Leave for smoking is a decision for the responsible clinician alone
_Why:_ The policy states that staff working on inpatient wards should not facilitate or accompany patients off site to smoke, explicitly including the use of Section 17 leave to facilitate smoking, and that staff should never permit smoking as a way to manage behaviour or reduce incidents.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Smoke Free Sites Policy (May 2025, issue 1, review January 2028)

**Q470. [Medium]** _Scenario: A patient asks whether they can use their trust vape in the day area._ What does the trust Smoke Free Sites Policy say about where patients can vape?
- A. Vaping is permitted anywhere on trust premises, indoors and outdoors
- **B. Vaping is permitted in outdoor areas across trust premises, and decisions about indoor use are made locally by each ward or service [correct]**
- C. Vaping is only permitted in designated smoking shelters
- D. Vaping indoors is permitted trust wide but banned outdoors near entrances
_Why:_ The policy says vaping is permitted in outdoor areas across trust premises, and that decisions around indoor use are made locally by each ward or service, balancing patient choice with safety, comfort and clinical needs.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Smoke Free Sites Policy (May 2025, issue 1, review January 2028)

**Q471. [Easy]** _Scenario: A visitor is found smoking in the ward garden._ How does the trust Smoke Free Sites Policy say breaches should be recorded?
- A. In the ward daily report book only
- **B. Using the trust's Datix reporting system [correct]**
- C. By email to the Health Protection Unit
- D. On the patient's SystmOne record only
_Why:_ The policy states that where a breach of the smoke free policy occurs it should be recorded using the trust's Datix reporting system, and that staff, patients and visitors involved should all be approached with understanding and professionalism.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Smoke Free Sites Policy (May 2025, issue 1, review January 2028)

**Q472. [Easy]** _Scenario: A patient asks if they can use the vape their partner brought in from home._ What does the Trust Approved Vape Standard Operating Procedure say?
- A. Personal vapes are allowed if they are checked by staff first
- B. Personal vapes are allowed but must be stored in the clinic room between uses
- **C. Only trust approved vape devices are to be used by service users within inpatient settings [correct]**
- D. Personal disposable vapes are allowed but rechargeable devices are not
_Why:_ Both the Smoke Free Sites Policy and the vape Standard Operating Procedure state that only trust approved vape devices are to be used by service users within inpatient settings, to ensure safety, prevent fire alarm activations and keep a consistent approach to care. The trust device is a pod style Totally Wicked Switz Pro.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Trust Approved Vape Standard Operating Procedure (No issue or review date shown on the document)

**Q473. [Easy]** _Scenario: A smoker is admitted at the weekend and accepts the offer of a trust vape._ What should they be issued with, and what happens to it at discharge?
- **A. One battery and one pack of prefilled pods, with the battery kept by the patient throughout admission and taken home on discharge [correct]**
- B. One battery and one pack of pods, both returned to the clinic room on discharge
- C. A week's supply of pods only, with the battery loaned from ward stock
- D. Two batteries and two packs of pods, all of which stay on the ward
_Why:_ The Standard Operating Procedure says patients who accept the offer should be given one battery and one pack of prefilled pods (two pods per pack) at the earliest opportunity after admission, and that the battery is kept by the patient throughout their admission and can be taken home on discharge.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Trust Approved Vape Standard Operating Procedure (No issue or review date shown on the document)

**Q474. [Hard]** _Scenario: You are checking the ward's vape supply at the start of a shift._ What stock level does the Trust Approved Vape Standard Operating Procedure say each ward holds, and where can patients buy more pods?
- A. A stock of five batteries and five packets of pods in the ward office, with more pods sold on the ward
- **B. A stock of two batteries and two packets of pods in the clinic room, with further pods purchased on site rather than on the ward [correct]**
- C. No ward stock at all, with every device ordered individually from pharmacy
- D. A stock of one battery per bed in the clinic room, with pods issued free on request
_Why:_ The Standard Operating Procedure describes a stock of two batteries and two packets of pods stored in the clinic room on each ward, replenished by the named on-site team for that unit once depleted. It states clearly that pods are not available to purchase on the ward, and patients buy further pods at the designated on-site outlet.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Trust Approved Vape Standard Operating Procedure (No issue or review date shown on the document)

**Q475. [Hard]** _Scenario: A patient admitted five days ago has not yet been spoken to about smoking._ What timescale does the Inpatient Smoking Cessation Service Standard Operating Procedure set for the offer of stop smoking support?
- A. Within 24 hours of admission
- **B. Within 7 days of admission, as an opt out offer [correct]**
- C. Within 28 days of admission
- D. At the first care programme approach review
_Why:_ The Standard Operating Procedure states that all inpatients are to be given an opt out offer of stop smoking support within 7 days of admission, and are to be visited at least once by a tobacco dependency advisor. Patients may opt out at any time, but those who do not are kept in regular contact until discharge or completion of treatment.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Inpatient Smoking Cessation Service Standard Operating Procedure (No issue or review date shown on the document)

**Q476. [Hard]** _Scenario: It is 3am and a newly admitted smoker is agitated and asking for nicotine replacement. No prescription is in place yet._ Which nicotine replacement therapy (NRT) products can nursing staff issue via the trust Homely Remedies Protocol?
- A. Any NRT product held in the clinic room
- **B. 16 hour nicotine patches (25mg and 15mg) and lozenges (4mg and 2mg) [correct]**
- C. Inhalators (15mg) and nicotine mouthspray (1mg) only
- D. 24 hour nicotine patches (21mg and 14mg) only
_Why:_ The Standard Operating Procedure lists 16 hour nicotine patches in 25mg and 15mg strengths and lozenges in 4mg and 2mg strengths as the products nursing staff can issue via the Homely Remedies Protocol. After that initial issue, further supplies should be issued on prescription, with the tobacco dependency advisor liaising with ward staff to get one in place.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Inpatient Smoking Cessation Service Standard Operating Procedure (No issue or review date shown on the document)

**Q477. [Medium]** _Scenario: A patient on the ward stops smoking after admission._ Which medicines does the trust smoking cessation Standard Operating Procedure name as affected by a change in smoking status, meaning prescribers must be told?
- A. Lithium, sodium valproate, sertraline and diazepam
- **B. Clozapine, olanzapine, theophylline and warfarin [correct]**
- C. Chlordiazepoxide, thiamine, haloperidol and quetiapine
- D. Metformin, ramipril, atorvastatin and aspirin
_Why:_ The Standard Operating Procedure names clozapine, olanzapine, theophylline and warfarin as medicines whose metabolism is affected by tobacco use, and requires prescribers to be informed at the earliest opportunity and kept updated on progress so doses can be adjusted.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Inpatient Smoking Cessation Service Standard Operating Procedure (No issue or review date shown on the document)

**Q478. [Medium]** _Scenario: A patient is transferred to the Royal Derby Hospital for physical health treatment and is screened for alcohol use there._ At what AUDIT-C score does the Alcohol Care Team (ACT) Standard Operating Procedure say the team will offer an assessment?
- A. Any score of 1 or above
- **B. A score of 5 or above [correct]**
- C. A score of 11 or above
- D. Only where the treating team makes a direct telephone referral
_Why:_ The Standard Operating Procedure states the ACT will offer an assessment to anyone scoring 5 or above on the AUDIT-C, which is completed on all inpatients aged 18 and over at the Royal Derby Hospital, with the referral also registered on Derbyshire Healthcare's SystmOne record.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Alcohol Care Team Standard Operating Procedure (December 2025, review April 2027)

## Trust Policy - Safeguarding (local process)

**Q479. [Easy]** _Scenario: You need to raise a safeguarding adults concern about a patient on the ward and are looking for the referral form._ Under the trust's Safeguarding Adults Policy and Procedure, where does the trust tell staff the links to safeguarding adults referral forms are held?
- A. In the paper policy folder in the ward office only
- B. They are only issued by the local authority once you have phoned them
- **C. On SystmOne, the Electronic Patient Record (EPR), which holds Safeguarding as a discrete Unit containing the relevant policy links [correct]**
- D. They are emailed out monthly by Learning and Development
_Why:_ The policy states that links to Safeguarding Adults referral forms are available on the SystmOne EPR, and that SystmOne has Safeguarding as a discrete Unit holding all relevant policy links including MAPPA, MARAC and Prevent.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Safeguarding Adults Policy and Procedure (May 2024, issue 7, review May 2027)

**Q480. [Medium]** _Scenario: A patient discloses recent financial abuse by a relative. The concern meets the safeguarding adults criteria._ On the trust's Safeguarding Adults Pathway for recent abuse, what timescale is given for making the referral and for putting a care plan or safety plan in place?
- **A. Same day [correct]**
- B. Within five working days
- C. Within 24 to 48 hours
- D. At the next Multidisciplinary Team (MDT) meeting
_Why:_ The trust's Safeguarding Adults Pathway (Recent Abuse) marks both the referral and the care plan / safety plan as same day actions.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Safeguarding Adults Policy and Procedure (May 2024, issue 7, review May 2027)

**Q481. [Hard]** The trust's Safeguarding Adults policy lists situations where an untoward incident form (Datix) must be completed as well as the safeguarding referral. Which of these is one of the listed examples?
- A. Any referral where the person has given their consent
- B. Any referral routed to Derby City rather than Derbyshire County
- C. A referral about a patient's partner where no incident has actually occurred
- **D. Alleged or actual abuse or mistreatment by anyone employed by the Trust, or working for the Trust in any role such as a contractor [correct]**
_Why:_ The policy's incident reporting section lists abuse by anyone employed by or working for the Trust as requiring a Datix alongside the referral, and adds that where the referral relates to a third party such as a partner or family member and no incident has occurred, a Datix may not be required.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Safeguarding Adults Policy and Procedure (May 2024, issue 7, review May 2027)

**Q482. [Medium]** _Scenario: You have discussed a concern with your team leader but you are both still unsure whether it meets the safeguarding threshold._ According to the trust's Safeguarding Adults Pathway, who should you seek advice from at that point?
- **A. The Multi-Agency Safeguarding Hub (MASH) for Derby City or social care for the county, or the Heads of Nursing / Safeguarding Lead [correct]**
- B. The Care Quality Commission
- C. The patient's GP, who will decide whether to refer
- D. The Trust's Learning and Development department
_Why:_ The 'Alert' box on the trust's pathway says that if you are still unsure you should seek advice via MASH (city) or social care, or from the Heads of Nursing / Safeguarding Lead.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Safeguarding Adults Policy and Procedure (May 2024, issue 7, review May 2027)

**Q483. [Hard]** Which multi-agency risk process does the trust's Safeguarding Adults policy name specifically for Derbyshire county, alongside the safeguarding referral route?
- A. Multi-Agency Risk Assessment Conference (MARAC)
- B. Multi Agency Public Protection Arrangements (MAPPA)
- **C. Multiagency Adult Risk Management (MARM) [correct]**
- D. The Channel panel
_Why:_ The policy names Multiagency Adult Risk Management (MARM) as the Derbyshire process, linked from the Derbyshire Safeguarding Adults Board, in addition to the safeguarding referral routes for city and county.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Safeguarding Adults Policy and Procedure (May 2024, issue 7, review May 2027)

**Q484. [Easy]** The trust's Safeguarding Standard Operating Procedure (SOP) for SystmOne describes two ways to open the Children and Adults Safeguarding Concerns template. What are they?
- **A. Via the Launch Pad (Safeguarding Concerns), or by right clicking the safeguarding node on the tree [correct]**
- B. Via the discharge summary or the handover sheet
- C. By emailing the safeguarding team or completing a paper form
- D. Only through the Datix incident reporting system
_Why:_ The SOP sets out both routes: select the patient and click Launch Pad then Safeguarding Concerns, or select the patient and right click Children and Adults Safeguarding Concerns on the tree.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Safeguarding Standard Operating Procedure (SystmOne) (Issue 01, no issue or review date recorded on the document)

**Q485. [Medium]** _Scenario: You have completed the Children and Adults Safeguarding Concerns template on SystmOne and clicked save._ What does the trust's Safeguarding Standard Operating Procedure (SOP) say you must do next?
- A. Set the entry to private so that only you can see it
- B. Add the ward manager as a co-author of the entry
- **C. Complete the Event Details box and make sure you click Safeguarding Relevant, then confirm the warning box that appears [correct]**
- D. Select the local authority as the recipient of the entry
_Why:_ The SOP is explicit that after saving you complete the Event Details box and ensure you click Safeguarding Relevant, then agree to the warning box that pops up.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Safeguarding Standard Operating Procedure (SystmOne) (Issue 01, no issue or review date recorded on the document)

**Q486. [Medium]** _Scenario: A note you wrote yesterday turns out to be safeguarding relevant, but it was not flagged at the time._ What does the trust's Safeguarding Standard Operating Procedure (SOP) say about this?
- A. The entry must be deleted and rewritten from scratch
- B. Nothing can be changed once an entry has been saved
- C. Only the safeguarding team can amend an entry after saving
- **D. An entry can be retrospectively marked as Safeguarding Relevant, by right clicking it in the tabbed journal and confirming [correct]**
_Why:_ The SOP says any entry relating to safeguarding should be saved as Safeguarding Relevant, and that an entry can be retrospectively marked as Safeguarding Relevant from the tabbed journal by right clicking it.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Safeguarding Standard Operating Procedure (SystmOne) (Issue 01, no issue or review date recorded on the document)

**Q487. [Medium]** Which situation does the trust's Safeguarding Children Procedures give as an example where an untoward incident form (Datix) must be completed as well as the safeguarding referral?
- **A. All incidents where a child has sustained a significant unexplained or suspicious injury [correct]**
- B. Any concern raised about a child's school attendance
- C. Any occasion where a child visits an adult inpatient ward
- D. Any referral where the family have given their consent
_Why:_ The trust's incident reporting section lists unexpected or suspicious child deaths, significant unexplained or suspicious injuries, all incidents involving a member of Trust staff, incidents relating to treatment or interventions, risk of FGM, slavery or honour based violence, and organisational abuse.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Safeguarding Children Procedures (May 2024, issue 2, Trust routine 3 yearly review)

**Q488. [Easy]** How does the trust expect staff to use the Derby and Derbyshire safeguarding children procedures?
- A. Print a copy at the start of the year and keep it in the ward folder
- **B. Use the web-enabled procedures on the day required rather than printing and saving them, because they are updated regularly [correct]**
- C. Rely on the printed version handed out at Trust induction
- D. Ask the ward manager to confirm the current wording each time
_Why:_ The procedures are the Derby and Derbyshire Safeguarding Children Partnership's web-enabled procedures, updated by the partnership as guidance changes, and the document strongly recommends using them for the day required rather than printing and saving, as they go out of date quickly. Staff are also asked to register for Tri-x alerts.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Safeguarding Children Procedures (May 2024, issue 2, Trust routine 3 yearly review)

**Q489. [Easy]** Which role holds the post of Trust Prevent Lead at this trust?
- A. The Medical Director
- B. The Head of Nursing for Adult Acute Inpatient Services
- C. The Chief Executive
- **D. The Assistant Director for Safeguarding Adults [correct]**
_Why:_ The trust's Prevent policy names the Assistant Director for Safeguarding Adults as the Trust Prevent Lead, working alongside the Trust Safer Care Co-ordinator (Prevent Co-ordinator) and the Assistant Director for Safeguarding Children.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Safeguarding Individuals Vulnerable to Extremism (Contest, Prevent and Channel) Policy and Procedure (January 2024, issue 5, review November 2026)

**Q490. [Hard]** _Scenario: It is 11pm and you identify what you believe is an immediate threat relating to radicalisation._ What does the trust's Prevent policy tell you to do out of hours?
- A. Complete a Datix and leave it for the safeguarding team to pick up on Monday
- B. Email the police Prevent Team and wait for a reply
- **C. Contact the First On-Call Manager and quote 'Channel', passing on all relevant information so they can raise the alert with Derbyshire Constabulary [correct]**
- D. Contact the local authority out of hours social care duty team
_Why:_ The policy says that out of hours staff should contact the First On-Call Manager and quote 'Channel'. The On Call Manager is then able to contact the relevant people at Derbyshire Constabulary, so all relevant information must be passed to them.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Safeguarding Individuals Vulnerable to Extremism (Contest, Prevent and Channel) Policy and Procedure (January 2024, issue 5, review November 2026)

**Q491. [Hard]** What does the trust's Prevent policy say happens after a referral is discussed at Channel Panel?
- A. The referring nurse records the outcome in the ward diary
- **B. The panel meets monthly, and the Trust Prevent Lead makes an entry on the Electronic Patient Record (EPR) summarising the discussion, within the Safeguarding Unit on SystmOne [correct]**
- C. No record is made, because Channel information cannot be held in health records
- D. The panel meets weekly and emails the outcome directly to the ward
_Why:_ The policy states that referrals are discussed at Channel Panel, which meets monthly, and that following the meeting the Trust Prevent Lead makes an EPR entry summarising the main points within the Safeguarding Unit on SystmOne. The panel decides whether the person meets the threshold for adoption to Channel.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Safeguarding Individuals Vulnerable to Extremism (Contest, Prevent and Channel) Policy and Procedure (January 2024, issue 5, review November 2026)

**Q492. [Medium]** Which two Prevent training packages does the trust's policy name as provided through Learning and Development?
- A. Prevent Level 1 and Prevent Level 2
- B. Safeguarding Level 3 and Safeguarding Level 4
- C. Contest Foundation and Channel Awareness
- **D. Basic Prevent Awareness Training (BPAT) and Workshop to Raise Awareness of Prevent (WRAP) [correct]**
_Why:_ The policy requires line managers to ensure staff attend the appropriate BPAT (Basic Prevent Awareness Training) and WRAP (Workshop to Raise Awareness of Prevent) training provided by the Trust's Learning and Development department. Non-clinical staff undertake Prevent e-learning, and all new staff receive Prevent information in the safeguarding presentation at Trust induction.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Safeguarding Individuals Vulnerable to Extremism (Contest, Prevent and Channel) Policy and Procedure (January 2024, issue 5, review November 2026)

**Q493. [Hard]** _Scenario: A detained patient managed under Multi Agency Public Protection Arrangements (MAPPA) is being considered for their first period of unescorted leave._ What does the trust's MAPPA policy require at this point?
- **A. A MAPPA I notification form completed or updated before first unescorted leave, and again after the first Care Programme Approach (CPA) or Care and Treatment Plan (CTP) meeting where discharge or release is discussed [correct]**
- B. Nothing until the point of discharge
- C. A notification only if the patient is detained under section 37/41
- D. A routine notification every three months regardless of leave status
_Why:_ The policy requires a MAPPA I form before first unescorted leave and after the first CPA or CTP meeting where discharge is discussed. It is also required before first escorted leave where there is a high risk of abscond, very high risk of harm or significant public interest, and before extended leave of absence. It goes to the MAPPA Co-ordinator for the local area and for the patient's home area.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Multi Agency Public Protection Panel (MAPPA) Policy and Procedure (May 2025, issue 4, review March 2028)

**Q494. [Medium]** Before referring a case for Multi Agency Public Protection Arrangements (MAPPA) level 2 or 3 management, what does the trust's policy recommend?
- A. Complete the MAPPA A referral form and let the ward manager decide
- **B. Complete a MAPPA Q screening form to check the case meets the threshold, and hold a discussion with the Assistant Director for Safeguarding Adults before the referral is made [correct]**
- C. Complete a MAPPA I form and discuss it with the responsible clinician
- D. Raise a Datix and let the on call manager decide
_Why:_ The policy recommends the MAPPA Q form for screening or thresholding a case, and a discussion with the Assistant Director for Safeguarding Adults before a referral is made. The Derbyshire MAPPA Unit then uses the National MAPPA Referral Form (MAPPA A).
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Multi Agency Public Protection Panel (MAPPA) Policy and Procedure (May 2025, issue 4, review March 2028)

**Q495. [Medium]** _Scenario: You have attended a Multi Agency Public Protection Arrangements (MAPPA) meeting about a patient and have been given the meeting paperwork._ What does the trust's MAPPA policy say about that paperwork and the clinical record?
- A. The MAPPA minutes must be scanned into the patient's record in full
- **B. MAPPA documents are restricted and should not be uploaded to clinical records, but a summary of the risks, the discussion and the actions should be entered into the clinical record after the meeting [correct]**
- C. Nothing about MAPPA may be recorded in the clinical record at all
- D. A clinical record must be opened for every person discussed at MAPPA
_Why:_ The policy says MAPPA documents are restricted and should not be uploaded to clinical records, while a summary of risks, discussion and actions should be entered after the meeting. It also states that if the person discussed does not already have a clinical record, there is no need to open one.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Multi Agency Public Protection Panel (MAPPA) Policy and Procedure (May 2025, issue 4, review March 2028)

**Q496. [Easy]** _Scenario: A restricted patient fails to return from a period of permitted leave._ Under the trust's MAPPA policy, who is responsible for informing the police, and when?
- A. The responsible clinician, on the next working day
- B. The MAPPA Co-ordinator, once the ward has notified them
- C. Nobody until the patient has been missing for 24 hours
- **D. The nurse in charge, immediately, passing the information to the local police force and including details of any known victims who may be at risk [correct]**
_Why:_ The policy places this duty on the nurse in charge for any restricted patient who escapes, absconds while on escorted leave, or fails to return from permitted leave, and says the information must be passed immediately to the local police force with information about any known victims at risk.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Multi Agency Public Protection Panel (MAPPA) Policy and Procedure (May 2025, issue 4, review March 2028)

**Q497. [Easy]** Under the trust's MAPPA policy, what should staff do when they identify an immediate threat?
- A. Record it in the notes and raise it at the next Multidisciplinary Team (MDT) meeting
- **B. Contact Derbyshire Constabulary and complete a Datix incident report, contacting the On Call Manager out of hours for support and advice [correct]**
- C. Contact the MAPPA Co-ordinator and take no further action
- D. Contact the Trust Caldicott Guardian for a decision
_Why:_ The Immediate Threats section of the policy directs staff to contact Derbyshire Constabulary and complete a Datix incident report, with the On Call Manager contacted out of hours for support and advice.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Multi Agency Public Protection Panel (MAPPA) Policy and Procedure (May 2025, issue 4, review March 2028)

**Q498. [Easy]** _Scenario: An allegation of sexually inappropriate behaviour is made against a member of staff._ What does the trust's Sexual Safety and Personal Boundaries policy require?
- A. A local management investigation first, with the police involved only if the allegation is substantiated
- B. A Datix only, since staff conduct is handled entirely by People Services
- **C. The incident must be reported to the Police and a safeguarding referral made, and the matter is investigated under the PIPOT and LADO processes and professional standards [correct]**
- D. The matter should be worked through in the staff member's clinical supervision
_Why:_ The policy states that all sexual safety incidents involving allegations against staff should be reported to the Police and a safeguarding referral made, and that any breach or potential breach of sexual boundaries between staff and a person using services will be investigated under the PIPOT (Person in a Position of Trust) and LADO processes, with the Police notified.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Sexual Safety and Personal Boundaries Policy and Procedure (June 2024, issue 2, review June 2027)

**Q499. [Hard]** _Scenario: You are writing up a sexual safety incident in the clinical record and on Datix._ Which term does the trust's Sexual Safety and Personal Boundaries policy tell staff to use for the person the allegation is about?
- A. Suspect
- **B. Person alleged to have caused harm [correct]**
- C. Perpetrator
- D. Offender
_Why:_ The policy's definition of 'allegation' sets out the presumption of innocence and states that when recording a sexual safety incident in clinical records or Datix, staff must use the terminology 'person alleged to have caused harm'.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Sexual Safety and Personal Boundaries Policy and Procedure (June 2024, issue 2, review June 2027)

**Q500. [Medium]** _Scenario: A patient discloses that they have just been sexually assaulted by another patient on the ward._ Which set of immediate actions matches the trust's Sexual Safety and Personal Boundaries policy?
- A. Wait until the Multidisciplinary Team (MDT) ward round the next morning before taking any action
- B. Ask both patients to write statements before contacting anyone else
- **C. Take immediate action to protect the person from further harm and establish the facts, prioritise any medical treatment needed, preserve evidence for forensic purposes as advised by the Police, obtain any CCTV footage available, and report via Datix [correct]**
- D. Move the patient to a different ward and hand the matter over at the next shift change
_Why:_ The policy requires immediate action to protect the adult at risk and establish the facts, says medical treatment must be prioritised, that evidence must be preserved for forensic purposes as requested or advised by the Police, that CCTV should be obtained if available, and that the incident is reported via Datix with all actions documented in the clinical record.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Sexual Safety and Personal Boundaries Policy and Procedure (June 2024, issue 2, review June 2027)

**Q501. [Hard]** Following a sexual safety incident, who does the trust's policy say should be involved in the safeguarding meeting that is held?
- A. The ward manager and the patient's named nurse only
- **B. The manager, Heads of Nursing and the Assistant Director of Safeguarding Adults, or Safeguarding Children if a child is involved, with consideration given to whether the matter sits within the PIPOT or LADO process [correct]**
- C. The Trust Board, who will decide what reporting is needed
- D. The Police alone, who decide who else needs to attend
_Why:_ The policy says a safeguarding meeting should be held involving the manager, Heads of Nursing and the Assistant Director of Safeguarding Adults (or Safeguarding Children if child related), that consideration will be given to whether it sits within PIPOT or LADO, and that actions from that meeting must be followed up including any reporting not already done.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Sexual Safety and Personal Boundaries Policy and Procedure (June 2024, issue 2, review June 2027)

**Q502. [Easy]** _Scenario: A newly admitted patient asks whether their eight year old daughter can come and visit._ Which trust form must the Multidisciplinary Team (MDT) complete following the initial assessment to record the decision about the visit?
- **A. The Children Visiting Mental Health Inpatient Areas Approval form, available on Focus under Approved Forms [correct]**
- B. A Datix incident report
- C. The Section 17 leave form
- D. The Family Feedback form for Children
_Why:_ The policy requires the Children Visiting Mental Health Inpatient Areas Approval form to be completed by the MDT following the initial assessment, and retained on the ward so that it can be picked up in the audit undertaken by the Heads of Nursing and Safeguarding Children Service.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Child Visiting to Mental Health Inpatient, Residential Areas and Community Policy and Procedure (June 2025, issue 04, review April 2028)

**Q503. [Medium]** _Scenario: You have been allocated to supervise and observe a child's visit to their parent._ What record does the trust's child visiting policy require you to complete?
- A. A verbal handover to the nurse in charge only
- B. A Datix for every child visit
- **C. The Observation of a visit by a child with a relative in a mental health inpatient or residential area form, which is then entered into the patient's clinical records [correct]**
- D. No record is needed unless something goes wrong
_Why:_ The policy requires appropriate and sensitive supervision so that the interaction between parent or carer and child can be observed, with the observation form (available on Focus under Approved Forms) completed by the observing staff member and entered into the patient's clinical records, and any concerns discussed at the MDT or with the Trust Safeguarding Children Service.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Child Visiting to Mental Health Inpatient, Residential Areas and Community Policy and Procedure (June 2025, issue 04, review April 2028)

**Q504. [Hard]** Where does the trust's child visiting policy say visits by children should take place?
- A. On the ward, in the patient's own bedroom
- **B. Outside of the ward, in an environment separate from other patients and appropriate to the child's needs, the only exception being The Beeches perinatal unit where children under 12 months may be admitted with their mother [correct]**
- C. In the ward dining room, during protected mealtimes
- D. Wherever the accompanying responsible adult prefers
_Why:_ The policy states that because of the potential for distress and aggression in a ward environment, visits with children need to take place outside of the ward and in an area separate from other patients, and names The Beeches (Perinatal Unit) as the exception where children under 12 months may be admitted with their mother. All inpatient services should provide or have access to a family friendly room.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Child Visiting to Mental Health Inpatient, Residential Areas and Community Policy and Procedure (June 2025, issue 04, review April 2028)

**Q505. [Medium]** _Scenario: A patient is admitted overnight and it is not yet known whether they have children._ By when does the trust's child visiting policy say information about children and family relationships must be collected, and by whom?
- A. Within the first week, by the named nurse
- B. At the first Care Programme Approach (CPA) review, by the care coordinator
- C. Only if the patient raises it themselves
- **D. On admission, or at the earliest opportunity within 24 hours, by the admitting nurse and doctor, as part of the initial care plan [correct]**
_Why:_ The policy states that on admission, or at the earliest opportunity within 24 hours, the admitting nurse and doctor must ensure information is collected about children and family relationships as part of the initial care plan. For informal admissions the admitting nurse also establishes who may visit and who holds parental responsibility.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Child Visiting to Mental Health Inpatient, Residential Areas and Community Policy and Procedure (June 2025, issue 04, review April 2028)

**Q506. [Hard]** _Scenario: The clinical team conclude that a child's visit would not be in that child's best interests._ Under the trust's child visiting policy, who makes that decision and how must it be recorded?
- A. The responsible clinician alone, recorded in the ward diary
- B. The Trust Safeguarding Unit, recorded on Datix
- **C. The multidisciplinary team, on the Decision To Refuse Child Visit Form, with the decision given in writing as well as verbally. In exceptional circumstances the nurse in charge of the ward may make the decision and must discuss it with the MDT at the first available opportunity [correct]**
- D. Children's Social Care, who issue a written direction to the ward
_Why:_ The policy says decisions to refuse visits will only be taken exceptionally, must be given in writing as well as verbally, must be supported by clear evidence from risk assessment and recorded on the Decision To Refuse Child Visit Form. The decision sits with the MDT, with the nurse in charge able to make it in exceptional circumstances and discuss it with the MDT at the first opportunity.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Child Visiting to Mental Health Inpatient, Residential Areas and Community Policy and Procedure (June 2025, issue 04, review April 2028)

**Q507. [Hard]** _Scenario: An incident occurs on the unit during a child's visit._ What escalation does the trust's child visiting policy set out?
- A. Tell the ward manager the following morning
- B. Inform Children's Social Care and take no other action
- C. Inform the visiting adult's GP
- **D. Notify the nurse in charge immediately and take any immediate action needed for the child's safety. The nurse in charge then informs the Bleep-holder or Night Co-ordinator, the Heads of Nursing and the Trust Safeguarding Children Service, and a Datix is completed [correct]**
_Why:_ The policy's inpatient incident management guidance requires immediate notification of the nurse in charge, who informs the Bleep-holder or Night Co-ordinator, Heads of Nursing and the Trust Safeguarding Children Service. The Bleep-holder informs the Service Line Manager, or the on call manager out of hours, and a Datix must be completed for any incident involving children during a visit.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Child Visiting to Mental Health Inpatient, Residential Areas and Community Policy and Procedure (June 2025, issue 04, review April 2028)

**Q508. [Easy]** _Scenario: A practitioner has just started in a service covered by the trust's safeguarding children supervision arrangements._ When does the policy say they should first be seen by a Safeguarding Children Named Nurse?
- A. At their first annual appraisal
- **B. As part of their induction, within one month of coming into post, to discuss their safeguarding children supervision and training needs [correct]**
- C. Only once they have a case they want to discuss
- D. Within six months of starting in post
_Why:_ The policy states that all Health Visitors, School Nurses, Nursery Nurses, Community Nurses, Children in Care Nurses, Specialist Nurses and CAMHS workers employed by the Trust should be seen by a Safeguarding Children Named Nurse as part of their induction within one month of coming into post.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Safeguarding Children Supervision Policy and Procedure (Oct 2023, issue 1, review Oct 2026)

**Q509. [Hard]** What minimum frequency does the trust's Safeguarding Children Supervision policy set for 1:1 safeguarding children supervision?
- A. Annually for everyone
- B. Monthly for everyone
- C. On request only, with no minimum stated
- **D. A needs-led basis every 3 to 6 months, with a minimum of 3 monthly for newly qualified staff and for team leaders or managers who hold safeguarding children supervision responsibilities [correct]**
_Why:_ The policy sets 1:1 supervision on a needs-led basis every 3 to 6 months, or sooner, with a minimum of 3 monthly for newly qualified staff and the same minimum for team leaders and managers with safeguarding children supervision responsibilities. The supervision agreement repeats the minimum of 3 to 6 monthly.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Safeguarding Children Supervision Policy and Procedure (Oct 2023, issue 1, review Oct 2026)

**Q510. [Medium]** _Scenario: You have just finished a safeguarding children supervision session in which two cases were discussed._ What does the trust's supervision agreement say about recording it?
- A. The supervisor records everything and the supervisee records nothing
- B. Records are kept only in the supervision file and never go into the child's record
- **C. The supervisee records the case discussion and action plan in the child's electronic patient record, and activity data is entered onto electronic records within two working days of the activity taking place [correct]**
- D. Recording is optional where no actions were agreed
_Why:_ The supervision agreement requires the supervisee to record the case discussion including the action plan in the child's EPR, to task the supervisor to review it if recorded after the session, and states that activity data must be entered onto electronic records within two working days. The supervisor separately records the discussion in the supervisee's file.
_Source:_ Derbyshire Healthcare - Safeguarding Children Supervision Policy and Procedure (Oct 2023, issue 1, review Oct 2026)

## Trust Policy - Dignity, Visiting & Carers

**Q511. [Easy]** _Scenario: Beds are tight across the unit and a duty manager suggests putting a male patient into a spare bed in a female bed bay overnight._ Under the trust's minimum standards for delivering same sex accommodation, how is overnight mixing of sexes in a bed bay rated?
- A. Green - acceptable if the nurse in charge agrees
- B. Amber - should not occur unless clinically unavoidable
- **C. Red - it should be a 'never event' and must not occur [correct]**
- D. Not rated, because bed pressures are outside the policy's scope
